## DP-13 — Play mode is not wall-locked and its FMV/load phases pay the emu quantum per in-window boundary: a `--frames 600 --harness-window` play leg reaches ≈303 frames in 120 s (2026-10-01, I7.6-A)

**Evidence.** With the play-mode ticker started (I7.6-A: `TickThread` notes a tick every 16667 us; `BoundaryProtocol::Wait` under Play fires the noted ticks on the guest
waiter) the frame-60 stall is gone (`RootPlayModeSmoke`: `frames=120 finalized=1 tick_thread=ticks=1391,resyncs=3 wall_ms=24438`), but the 600-frame play smoke the stage was
asked for does not finish inside its 120 s bound: `<work>/int_work/i76a/play600.log` `[run_bounded] wall timeout 120s -- killing pid 34221`,
`play600/frames.fhash` 303 lines. Two timing-domain facts, neither this stage's to change: (1) the Play policy as Phase 3 wrote it does NOT park the boundary for the pacer's
tick (`BoundarySequencer` slot 1 fires its own Boundary tick; `tick_source.h`: "the host pacer sleeps PeriodUs() between ticks and calls Tick()"), so gameplay boundaries run
as fast as the host (I7.1-A's INT-I71A-PLAY-RING-OUTPACES-DEVICE), while (2) every kernel timer wait and every libmpeg pace park (`mpeg_hooks.park` → `protocol.Wait`) is a
REAL 16.67 ms wait per demanded tick, and `EmuDomain::OnRawTick` absorbs the demanded ticks while `InLoop` (≤ `kIdleReleaseTicks` = 6 raw ticks after a boundary) once the
frame quantum is exhausted — so a 2-tick FMV pace park that follows an in-FMV boundary costs ≈7 wall ticks (≈117 ms per picture), and the title's load-wait laps (DP-1's
T0-paced loop) cost their full real duration (OLD measured 33 s loads before its load turbo). OLD's FMV loop raised no boundary (DP-12), so OLD paid the 6-tick absorption once
per movie, not once per picture.

**Options.** (a) wall-lock the Play boundary as OLD's `waitForNextVBlankTick` did (slot 1 parks until the pacer's next noted tick instead of firing its own Boundary tick) —
the full OLD play pacing, a Play-policy-only change to `BoundarySequencer::InstallPhase3Steps` / `BoundaryProtocol`; (b) under Play only, let the ticks a guest WAITER fires
(the `Deferred` cause from `BoundaryProtocol::Wait`) bypass the quantum absorption (`EmuDomain::OnRawTick` treats a waiter-fired tick as the idle-release form), so a pace park
of N ticks costs N wall ticks; (c) bind `TurboGovernor` (load turbo, `cdvd.load_turbo`, OLD's 33 s → 16 s) in play mode so the load laps shorten; (d) accept play-mode FMV/load
phases at real duration and size play smokes by wall time (the `--frames 120` pin), not by a frame count that spans them.

**Recommendation.** (d) now (the pin is `--frames 120`, 24 s; nothing in the deterministic legs is touched); (a)+(b) together as the Play-policy half of plan row 6.7's
present-policy decision (one timing-domain change, pinned by a play smoke that must hold 60 boundaries/s ± the present's cadence); (c) after (a).
**User decision (2026-10-01 19:19):** (a)+(b) approved NOW, not deferred — wall-lock the Play boundary to the pacer's vblank tick and land row 6.7's present policy (present every boundary at the display rate; FMV/load phases included). Play-mode only; deterministic/headless legs must stay byte-identical (pin that). Do this right after DP-8 (1).
**Status: IMPLEMENTED (I8.10-A, 2026-10-02)** — (a) `BoundarySequencer::BindPacer(&protocol)` (root, iff play mode): the TickAdvance slot parks for the pacer's next noted tick (`BoundaryProtocol::ParkForPacerTick`, bounded 2 periods, a stopped pacer falls back to one Boundary tick) and fires the FIRST noted tick as the boundary's field instead of its own Boundary tick; a late boundary (tick already noted) does not park, counted (`pace_late`), and its SURPLUS noted ticks (the pacer ticked again while the EE was busy) are routed through `EmuDomain::OnRawTick`'s quantum rule — absorbed inside the loop, 1:1 past the idle-release window, never burst-repaid (OLD `ps2_vblank.cpp:1341-1366` paced branch: park until `g_vblank_count != current`; `:1370-1375` "ticks that FIRED while the EE was busy … are host-stall time, not game time", implemented there by `time_domain.h:218-231`'s absorb; the ticker's `:1185-1188` re-anchor); counted `pace_surplus`. (b) `EmuDomain::OnDeferredTick` with `SetPacerRelease(true)` (bound with the pacer): the tick a guest WAITED for (the boundary's field, a parked kernel-timer waiter's ticks) rides 1:1. Row 6.7: `engine/gs/hle/present/present_policy.{h,cpp}` (`PresentPolicy`: live/hold/none, `video.fps_cap` bound) consulted at the Verify slot's present site in play mode only. Deterministic legs byte-identical: `RootPlayPolicyInert` (coldboot 130: `mem.mhash`/`audio.ahash`/`frames.fhash` line-identical to the pre-change fixture `tests/app/fixtures/coldboot_130_lanes.txt`; mem `0x03018fa3110d34b5/0x2c5b6d50e6b67abf/0x18ce63c76bf63e84`). Unit pins `PacerReleaseDomain`, `PacerReleaseSequencer`, `PresentPolicyTable`; root play pin `RootPlayWallLock` (pacing measured: `pace_parks`/`pace_late`/`wall_ms`). Census `pace_parks=`, `pace_late=`, `pace_timeouts=`, `pace_surplus=`, `domain=tick=,absorbed=,regime=`, `present_policy=`. (c) TurboGovernor in play mode untouched (the user's).

**FMV phase of row 6.7 (orchestrator, 2026-10-02 14:40, after the user's report "the FMVs are not running, at most a single frame of the second one"):** the window was re-presented only at the EE boundary's PresentRendezvous slot, and a movie never crosses the EE frame loop (547 pictures inside ~10 boundaries; in play mode 535/547 pictures were `late`), so the display showed one picture per boundary. Landed, play mode only: (1) the composition root's DISPLAY TICK — a VSync job (`arg1 = 2`, no EE frame mark) through the sink chain on every field the FMV pace park waits through and on every serve no park follows (`LibmpegBindings::Hooks::on_served`), census `display_ticks=<n>,<at-serve>`; (2) `FmvPacer::SetReanchorPeriods(4)` via `MpegApi::SetPacerReanchorPeriods` — opt-in bounded catch-up: a grid ≥ 4 periods behind re-anchors at `now` (slow motion, every picture shown) instead of draining the backlog as a blur (movie 2 ran in 0.8 s before); off in deterministic legs (the reference contract and its ported tests unchanged), census `fmv_pacer_reanchors=`. Evidence: play leg `--frames 200` `display_ticks=547-class` (pin `RootPlayFmvPresents`: display_ticks ≥ served_total − 20), both movies 10.8 s → 34.0 s wall (≈ 1.25× slower than authored: the per-picture EE cost ≈ 41 ms, the I8.10-B DecodeFallback class); deterministic coldboot 200 frames byte-identical to leg190b (frames 190/190, mem 4/4, audio windows 0–31; `fmv=…late=6,parks=536,park_ticks=1071` unchanged, `display_ticks=0,0`).

## DP-14 — `--vu1-library none` (the VU1 HLE-off switch, PHASE5_IMPL (c)/(e) [GD1]) has no Config path: `engine.vu1_mode` cannot express it without changing the deterministic lanes (2026-10-01, I5.3-A)

**Evidence.** PHASE5_IMPL (c) is MANDATORY: `hle_enabled = (library != none)`, with `engine.vu1_mode` mapping `Hle → Cached, Cached → Cached, Interp → Interp` — the mode selects the
FALLBACK tier, the library selects the consult. The deterministic profile pins `vu1_mode = Cached` and every frozen NEW lane was recorded with the title library consulted under it
(`int_work/i53a/pre/coldboot130/run.meta`: `vu1_hle=318/0/0`; the artdet anchor: `vu1_hle=37819/162/0`). So reading `Cached` as "HLE off" (the prompt's candidate) would turn
the consult off on every harness leg; reading `Hle` as the only HLE-on value collapses two enum values onto one tier with different consults and still leaves the harness legs
changed. Neither existing value expresses `none` without touching the lanes; the CLI is parsed inside `ConfigLoader::Load` (I5.1-A INT-I51A-VU1-LIBRARY-SWITCH), so a
`--vu1-library` flag is a Config schema change — a MUST-STOP. I5.3-A therefore binds `hle_enabled = true` for all three modes (the only library the root links is the title's;
run.meta `vu1_library=title`, `vu1_tier=cached|interp`) and leaves the lanes byte-identical.

**Options.** (a) a new key `engine.vu1_library = title | none` (Config schema + `Fingerprint`; the deterministic profile pins `title`; `--set engine.vu1_library=none` is the [GD1]
switch); (b) a CLI-only flag `--vu1-library none` surfaced through `ConfigLoader::Load` into `harness.*`-class plumbing (no schema field, no fingerprint change; refused in files
like the harness keys); (c) re-purpose `Vu1Mode::Hle` as the ONLY consult-on value and change the deterministic profile to `Hle` (lanes unchanged in bytes, but every recorded
`profile=deterministic` run.meta / Fingerprint changes and `Cached` silently becomes HLE-off).

**Recommendation.** (b): the switch is a tool proof knob (tiers vs library), never a shipped setting; it belongs beside `--headless`/`--frames`, not in the schema. (a) if the
switch must be reachable from `ps2r.ini`.
**User decision (2026-10-01 19:19):** (b) approved — CLI knob beside --headless/--frames, not a Config key.
**Status: IMPLEMENTED (I8.1-A, 2026-10-02)** — `--vu1-library <title|none>` parsed in `ConfigLoader::Load` (`engine/core/config/config_loader.{h,cpp}`; `Result::vu1_library_none`, the process-wide `ConfigLoader::HarnessRequest` the root reads; refused values are a Load error; no Config field, Fingerprint unchanged); the root maps `none` → `VuSettings::hle_enabled = false` and does not set the `Vu1HleRuntime` hook; `run.meta vu1_library=title|none`. Pin ctest `RootVu1LibraryNone` (artdet 2-frame: `vu1_library=none`, `vu1_hle=0/0/0`, finalized; control leg `title` with runs > 0).

## DP-15 — Three OLD-only ≈0x6168-byte payload images at the top of EE RAM (`0x1f00000`, `0x1f40000`, `0x1f80000`; DP-8 class (3)) are OLD's PTC-STUB view slots — proposed §7.1 row + masks (2026-10-02, I8.3-G)

**Evidence.** The writer is named: OLD's recompiler-emitted PTC-STUB hook (`OLD/recompiler_tool/recompiler.cpp:8440-8560`, emitted into OLD's generated code) runs whenever a `.ptc` open fails (`is_ptc_path && data == 0`): `STUB_BASE = 0x01F00000`, `STUB_STRIDE = STUB_SIZE = 0x40000`, `slot = s_stub_slot++ % 4`, and it copies the synthesized `.ptc` stream (`GetPtcRecord(norm_path)`, built by `InitArchiveTable()` in `hle_heap.cpp` from `Startup\Decal.ptc` with per-path hash patches) byte by byte into guest RAM (`memory::write<uint8_t>(stub_ptr + i, (*rec)[i])`); OLD reserves the region for it (`ps2_threading.cpp:1007-1021` "Reserve the top 1 MB of EE RAM (0x01F00000..0x02000000) for the PTC-STUB 256-KB view slots", `[ps2_threading] Reserving 0x100000 bytes at 0x1f00000 for PTC-STUB views`). The observed heads match the emitted per-path hashes: `0x1f40000` = `c9 59 ce f7 71 a5 0e 90` = `stub_hash_a/b 0xF7CE59C9/0x900EA571` (`Startup\Particle1.ptc`), `0x1f80000` = `93 da 44 fa 31 a2 2a ac` = `0xFA44DA93/0xAC2AA231` (`Particle2.ptc`); `0x1f00000` (slot 0, inside the `0x01effef0-0x01f06168` region of I4.10-L, head `AMERICAN.MB` string = DP-4 (b)'s scratch at `0x1effef0` + the slot-0 stream from `0x1f00000`) = `Particle0.ptc`. Retail: the particle records live inside `Default.rm2` and are served by the title's own archive reader (NEW serves them through the recompiled STREAM.IRX; its `.ptc` hashmap entries at `0xbd0bb4…` carry the real records — `0xbd0bb4` OLD `ffd857f5` (the stub's Particle0 hash) vs NEW `93da44fa`); nothing on hardware writes `0x1f00000-0x1fc0000`. Observed at boundary 120 (`int_work/i83g/regions_b120.out`): `0x01f00000-0x01f06168` (slot 0), `0x01f40000-0x01f46168` (6180 words), `0x01f80000-0x01f86168` (6180 words), OLD payload / NEW zeros; slot 3 (`0x1fc0000`, Decal.ptc) not yet written at 120. Consequence words (NOT maskable, attributed): the title's particle hashmap entries whose record ORDER/hashes differ (`0xbd0bb4-0xbd14e8`, 261 words at 120) because OLD registers the stub's synthesized records.

**Options.** (a) §7.1 row `P4-CKPT120-OLD-PTC-STUB-SLOTS` ("OLD writes synthesized .ptc streams into a reserved top-of-RAM region retail never touches") + mask `0x01f00000-0x01fc0000` (the four 256-KB slots; HI exclusive) — the slot contents are host data, not guest state; the hashmap consequence words stay unmasked and attributed. (b) Row, no mask (attribute the ≈18.5 k words at every boundary ≥ 120). (c) Narrow masks per observed slot extent (`0x1f00000-0x1f06168`, `0x1f40000-0x1f46168`, `0x1f80000-0x1f86168`) — brittle (the extent is the record size, which varies per path).

**Recommendation.** (a). An "OLD is wrong" claim with a mask → MUST-STOP; nothing applied (the DP-4 (b-1) 6-word mask covers only the scratch string).

## DP-16 — The anchors no longer finish 600 frames inside `run_leg.py`'s default 120 s bound after DP-8 (1): artdet 192 frames in 112 s (was 600 in 31 s) — the un-seamed headroom predicate lets the title's work-bounded maintenance loops run to their bound every slice (2026-10-02, I8.3-G)

**Evidence.** `verify/battery.py … --reps 1` (`int_work/i83g/battery/`): every anchor leg is killed by the per-leg bound (`[run_bounded] wall timeout 120s -- killing pid …`, `run_leg.py` default `timeout = max(120, 60 + frames // 10)`): artdet `frames=192 wall_ms=111758 finalized=0`, f36935 `frames=128 finalized=0`, … (per-leg table in the I8.3-G record). Before DP-8 (1) the same legs ran 600 frames in 30–56 s (I4.10-L `artdet wall_ms=30113`, `f40391 56048`; I5.G5 `30907`). The coldboot leg is unaffected (1500 frames in 143.7 s). Mechanism (not yet traced): with `FUN_00191bd0` recompiled over the slice-frozen T0 the headroom predicate is CONSTANT inside a slice; the three loader sites get their exits from `LoaderSpinLift`, but the title's other headroom-gated maintenance loops (the two other managers' sites and the lap-end query `0x17d87c`, `loader_spin_lift.h:6-15`; I4.10-M "the maintenance loops' other six sites are work-bounded") now iterate until their own work bound instead of until OLD's budget `rem` (which shrank 96 counts per query) ran out — more guest work per slice, deterministic (the images are reproducible) but ≈4–6× slower on the anchors, where every frame is a gameplay frame with a full maintenance pass. This is the RISK I4.10-M named for option (i) ("inside a slice the predicate is constant … the maintenance loops' other sites") materialising as throughput, not as a stall.

**Evidence (anchor what-if, same stage).** OLD artdet leg with `PS2_BUDGET_DIAG` (`int_work/i83g/old_art_diag.log`, image = baseline `0x1f6b66dd323e0c8a`): OLD's budget model answers **`hr=0` on the first THREE headroom queries of EVERY frame** and `hr=1` only on the fourth (`[BUDGET-EMU] #4 ra=17d724 f12=0.0016953 … rem=704 hr=0 vf=37157 dpf=365 ph=0`, `#5 … rem=608 hr=0`, `#6 … rem=512 hr=0`, `#7 ra=17d8ac f12=0.0006781 … rem=416 hr=1`; the same pattern at vf 37156–37160, `dpf=341–365` keeps the base at 8800). NEW's recompiled predicate over the slice-frozen T0 answers headroom to all four (elapsed 0 → remaining 9600). Consequence measured at boundary 2 (`old_art/state_b1` ≙ b2 `0xd3a840d1cd3d9330` vs `new_art/state_b2` `0x4b731e3313d46e6f`; `wd_art_b2.out`): the clock words are now IDENTICAL (`0xb97668 1b821700`, `0x309d04 3c888888`, `0x323534 006d78d6` on both) and the HEAP class shrank from 38,663 (I5.G4/G5) to **20,050 RAM0 words** (SPAD 705, META 0) — but the display list still grows at NEW's slice 2 (`0x323360` cursor `0x4f1e00` vs OLD `0x4e9390`; region `0x4e8f60-0x4f1dfc` 6173 words), the float/animation blocks (`0x3fd520` 1187, `0xad4750` 452, `0x723f20…` 4 × 280), the fade `0xbd3560` (1113 words, `c0c0c000` vs `c0c0c008` — NEW one fade step ahead) and six 262-word float tables (`0xbd2560…` OLD at their initial constants, NEW evaluated) remain: NEW performs the headroom-gated per-frame work that OLD's model skips. I5.G6's attribution of the display-list growth to the 5× clock is therefore FALSIFIED (the clock is equal, the growth persists); the class is the DP-3 mechanism on gameplay frames — **neither side is hardware-proven**: OLD's synthetic base is not a clock, NEW's frozen-T0 predicate never sees the frame's own elapsed work. The same mechanism is the throughput symptom above (every headroom-gated pass runs every frame).

**Options.** (a) evidence stage: lldb/`run.meta` count of headroom-query calls per site per slice on one anchor (which loop runs to its bound), then (b) a per-slice headroom model for the predicate: T0 advances inside a slice by the emulated cost of the work done (DP-1 (b) / DP-8 (ii) — a timing-domain redesign, MUST-STOP); or (c) extend `LoaderSpinLift`'s outcome-invariance guards to the other sites (DP-8 (iv); only if their outcomes are shown invariant); or (d) accept the throughput and raise the harness bound (`run_leg.py --timeout`, `battery.py` has no knob today) — hides nothing (the lanes are deterministic) but the anchors' 600-frame legs then take ≈6 min each; (e) hardware evidence (PCSX2 T0 trace of the four queries on a gameplay frame) to decide which answers the console gives.

**Recommendation.** (d) at once for the gate runs (a `--timeout` pass-through in `battery.py`, harness-only) so the §4.5 gate can measure 600 frames; (a) in the same stage; (b)/(c)/(e) are the user's — the anchors' remaining 20 k-word class and the throughput share this decision.

**Status (d): IMPLEMENTED (I8.4-A, 2026-10-02)** — `verify/battery.py --timeout S` passes `--timeout S` through to `run_leg.py` for every NEW leg (default behaviour unchanged when absent; `RB_STALL_S` untouched; `battery.py --selftest` pins the argv; documented in `verify/README.md`). The same bound was needed by this stage's own `--state-every 1` leg: 190 frames with 183 state writes were killed at the default 120 s (`[run_bounded] wall timeout 120s`), finalized with `--timeout 600` (`wall_ms=144624`). (a)/(b)/(c)/(e) untouched — the user's.

Guest EE code outside the DMAC write ≈ 2.9 %. The pipeline runs INLINE on the EE thread inside the GIF kick's `Dmac::Write32` (`gs/hle/queue/README.md`: the RenderQueue/graphics-thread consumer is "Later (not written)"). *Hit ratio (lldb counters, 8 frames, steady state frames 1–7):* 1609 draws/frame, `DecodeFallback` 1429/frame (89 % of draws reach the decode tier; the rest are untextured or target serves), `DecodeCache::L2Lookup` 28/frame (= L1 misses: **L1 hit 98.0 %**), `DecodeAndUpload` **9.4/frame (0.66 %; L1+L2 hit 99.3 %)**, `VramImage::ReadView` 8350/frame (5.8 per `DecodeFallback`), `OnCpuWrite` 31/frame (`FRAME consumer_frames=7 consumer_draws=13209 ResolveRead=13209 DecodeFallback=11770 L2Lookup=232 DecodeAndUpload=100 ReadView=69229`). **The cache is not missing; the keying and invalidation are sound.** *Cause — `DecodeFallback` (`engine/gs/hle/texsrc/texture_resolver.cpp:614-711`) does its decode-input preparation BEFORE the cache lookup, on every call, hit or miss* (per call-site line of the 1915 samples): `:708` function exit = destructors of the locals `window`, `clut`, `mip_win`, `mip_in` **582 (23.6 % of the EE thread)**; `:639` `vram_.ReadView(0, VramImage::kBytes, view_.data())` = a **4 MiB memcpy of ALL of VRAM per textured draw** (`vram_image.cpp:187-206`; 1429 × 4 MiB ≈ 5.6 GiB/frame) **374 (15.1 %)** — `view_` is read only by `DecodeAndUpload` (a miss); `:654` `mip_win[i].resize(L.fp_size)` 334 (13.5 %); `:658` `ContentKey::Compute` (xxHash64 of the footprint window — the content key by design, `content_key.h`) 290 (11.7 %); `:642` `std::vector<uint8_t> window(win_size)` 171 (6.9 %); `:646` `clut.resize` 127 (5.1 %); `:682` `DecodeAndUpload` 13 (0.5 %). The vector construct/destroy cost is the Debug preset's per-byte `allocator_traits::construct/destroy` loops (`CMakePresets.json` `macos-dev`: `CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE Debug`; self time: `_platform_memmove` 15.6 %, `allocator_traits<…unsigned char>::destroy` 10.6 %, `::construct` 8.6 %, `__split_buffer<unsigned char>::__construct_at_end` 6.2 %, `vector<unsigned char>::__base_destruct_at_end` 5.1 %, `ps2r::hash::` 10.8 %). **Class: a CONSTRUCTION item (Phase-6 performance), not a keying/invalidation bug and not inherent decode cost** — a pure-hoist fix changes no key and no pixel: (1) move the full-VRAM `ReadView` into the miss path (`DecodeAndUpload`), or better let the decoder read the footprint window already copied; (2) make `window`/`clut`/`mip_win` persistent scratch members like `view_`/`texels_` (resize, never reallocate) or hash straight from one footprint copy; pin = the same `frames.fhash`/`drawir.ohash` on `new_gl30` plus `wall_ms/frames` < 300 on the 30-frame artdet leg (or a `ReadView` byte counter in run.meta ≤ footprint bytes per draw). Expected after (1)+(2): the per-draw cost falls to the footprint copy + hash (≈ 12 % of today's EE thread) → ≈ 200 ms/frame in Debug; the remaining hash cost is the content-key design's price (a tuple → key short-circuit keyed on the footprint's upload epoch, `WindowEpoch`, would remove it — a design choice, listed, not proposed). The 6.3 queue (GS work off the EE thread) is the plan row for the structural half and is not needed to recover this item. No decision needed; BUG row proposed in the I8.10-B record.**

## DP-17 — The coldboot audio onset at W142 (OLD non-silent, NEW silent to W249): it is the boot state machine's case 6 → 7 edge (title screen + STREAM.IRX music voices), which NEW never reaches — NEW's STREAM.IRX CD-read path goes idle (124 IOP reads vs OLD's 334 before the edge); the FMV PCM is mixed at BVOL 0 on BOTH sides and is not the class (2026-10-02, I8.4-B)

NEW `fmv_pcm=…mixed=585120…bvol=0x0/0x0` (`SpuCore::MixInto` adds the external input at the core-0 BVOLL/BVOLR shadows, both 0): W28–W141 are silent on BOTH sides (NEW's gain is the BVOL shadow 0 at the leg's end; OLD's zero rms while `fmv_c` grows points at the same gain, inferred — the title's BVOL programming on this path is not traced here); OLD-equal, so not a NEW gap. *NEW never reaches case 7:* `find_bootsm.py` on I8.3-G's images: `state_b120 0x98c140 word=0x60204000010000 case=2` (= OLD's b120 image `case=2`), `state_b180 case=5`, `state_b240 case=6`; the leg's end (`cb/run.meta`, 1500 boundaries): `snd_kon=0`, `audio_first_nonsilent_window=none`, `vu1_hle=2998/0/0` (= OLD's `native runs=3000` just BEFORE its edge), `iop_cdvd=reads=124,sectors=195`, `iop_threads=1:Wait/Sleep,2:Wait/Sema` — NEW sits in case 6 from ≤ b240 to b1500 while OLD leaves it after ≈ 400 boundaries; OLD's STREAM.IRX issued **334** IOP `sceCdRead`s (257 × 2048 B, 29 × 4096, 19 × 6144, …) before the edge and the two 256 KB music reads at it; NEW's module issued 124 reads (195 sectors) in 1500 boundaries and its two threads are asleep. Case 6 is the front-end load (the `StartUp\Frontend…` path strings NEW holds at `0xbc3620` at b120; `[CHUNK-APPLY-SUM] rel{drain=7438 …}`, `[BUDGET-LIFT] disengage after 5.14s` in OLD): its completion is served through the module's CD reads, which stop on NEW.

**Class.** NEW construction gap in the recompiled STREAM.IRX ↔ IOP CDVD read path (the I5.G7/G8 "no CD reads after …" class seen on the anchors, now on coldboot): the module's CD worker stops issuing reads, the front-end load never completes, the boot machine parks in case 6, so no title screen, no music, W142+ silent. Not OLD-wrong (OLD's onset is the retail sequence); not the audio tap (the mixer and the hash see every voice; `snd_kon=0` is the fact). **Options.** (a) attribution stage on the IOP side: trace the module's `sceCdRead`/`sceCdStRead` calls and the worker thread's last wait on NEW (the `iopsched` diag registry entry exists; a per-call `run.meta` census) and compare with OLD's 334-read sequence (`old_cb.log` `[IOP-THREAD] sceCdRead` lines, lba/size) to find the first missing read; (b) defer behind the §4.4 stopping rule (W28; the walk's checkpoint 120 is before the stall). **Recommendation.** (a) — it is the boot's progression blocker, not an audio item. No decision.

**Attribution (a) (I8.5-A, 2026-10-02, evidence only — `int_work/i85a/`: `mgr_dump.py` (boot-SM object + loader manager pending list + STREAM slot bytes from `.ps2st` images), `iso_lba_map.py`, `cb300/` (NEW coldboot 300 frames, states every 60), `cb900/` (900 frames, states every 300), `old_case6_reads_per_frame.txt`, `diff_cb900.out`; OLD read only — `tmp_analysis/gh_dump/{00177168,00173ce8,001681f8,00172d88}.dec.txt`, `host_app/main.cpp:4204-4262`, `host_app/hle_heap.cpp:2130-2175, 2696-2760`, `host_app/memory.cpp:3148-3340`, and I8.4-B's `old_cb.log`/`old_cb_runtime_log.txt`):** **the class above is wrong in its mechanism: nothing is unwired and no module read is missing — the boot machine is not parked, it is crawling at NEW's delivery clock.** *(1) The case-6 exit condition (OLD recomp, Ghidra):* `FUN_00177168` case 6 → `FUN_00173ce8`; after the fade (`FUN_00172d88(obj, 0x29)` ≠ 0) it returns 7 iff `FUN_001681f8(obj+0x4f0, 1|2, 0)` ≠ 0, i.e. `FUN_002aa7f8(mgr=*0x309738, key=obj+0x500)` finds the level entry BY NAME in the manager's pending list (`mgr+0x18`) and `FUN_002ac8b0(entry, 1)` (and `FUN_002a9748` for the children) sees its two sub-resources at load state ≥ 4 (OLD `memory.cpp:3295-3310` `[BEACH-GATE]`: "PCSX2 mid-load: beach sub0=3 (read in progress), sub1=4 → it escapes the instant sub0 hits 4"). The key is **`Levels\Earth\Hub\Beach`** — the title screen is the Beach hub scene, not a "front-end" file; the condition is a HOST-SERVED LOADER completion (`LoaderFileIo`/`LoaderModel`), not a STREAM.IRX reply, semaphore or CD status. *(2) NEW's state (`mgr_dump.py`, identical in I8.3-G's `cb/` and this stage's `cb300/`/`cb900/` images — the leg is deterministic):* b180 `case=5 … head18=0` (no load yet); **b240 `case=6 ctr=716 entry=666 marker14=703 cnt504=22 key500='Levels\Earth\Hub\Beach'`, pending list `entry 0xc2f560 'levels\earth\hub\beach' st=22 sub0=0xc1a9a0(st2) sub1=0xc1aa80(st2)`; b300 and b600 `sub0 … (st3 nib3) sub1 … (st3 nib0)`** — the subs advanced 2 → 3 (reads in progress) and sit there while the reads trickle in; `slot_status` (STREAM client bytes `0x3b3d00..`) all 0 — no module-owned record pending. *(3) The rate is the whole story:* `run.meta` `loader_reads=5/918/3138916` at b300, `5/1518/5548343` at b900, `5/2118/9755552` at b1500 (I8.3-G) — **600 reads per 600 boundaries, exactly ONE host-served loader read delivered per EE boundary**, with `loader_spin_lifts=lap1=11,lap2=15` → `611,615` → `1211,1215` (+1 lap-1 and +1 lap-2 lift per boundary: each frame the title issues the next window read, polls it pending, the headroom lift ends the frame, the boundary delivers it, the next frame issues the next). OLD in case 6 (`old_cb_runtime_log.txt` between the first `[177168-SWITCH] … case=6` and the first `case=7`, `LC_ALL=C awk`): **403 frames, 4,679 `[DEFERRED-READ] Timer expired` completions — 171 frames with 0 (the fade/transition), then 22–23 completions per frame (128 frames × 23, 31 × 22)**, each as `[HLE READ] ASYNC — deferring N bytes` → `[LDSM]` poll → `[DEFERRED-READ] Timer expired, completing` → the next `[HLE READ]` INSIDE the same frame. OLD's in-frame delivery site is a park-loop pump: the `FUN_002b4738` ("isDone?" loader-SM poll) dual hook (`main.cpp:4210-4262`) runs `maint::RunGuestDuties` (→ `ProcessDeferredIsoReads`) every 64 polls once the poll has repeated 4,096 times within one `vf`, and under `PS2_CDVD_INSTANT` a deferred read's floor is one pump pass (`hle_heap.cpp:2722` `dr.delay_ticks = CdvdInstantOn() ? 1 : 3; dr.due_ms = 0`; the deterministic profile's `cdvd.latency_scale=0` turns the DVD model off). NEW's `DeliveryMeter` (`engine/cdvd/delivery_meter.h`: "Instant — a completion is visible at the NEXT boundary after its issue tick"; Modelled: "never earlier than the next boundary") has no in-slice visibility by design, and `LoaderModel::ShouldYieldPollSlice` is built on exactly that ("the read cannot land inside the slice (boundary-only delivery)"). *(4) The "124 vs 334" module reads are not a gap:* OLD's 334 IOP `sceCdRead`s before the edge = 122 in case 1 (`DATA.BD` +1474..+1617 sectors; NEW's module issued the same 124/195 sectors — `iso_lba_map.py`: lba 477162/477581 → `/DATA/DATA.BD`) + 212 in case 6 at lba 527124–532250 (`DATA.BD` +51436..+56562, from vf 1176 = 206 presents AFTER the case-6 entry) — module-side stream buffers the title registers only once the Beach load has progressed past the point NEW's crawl has reached by b1500; the module's two threads are idle (`iop_threads=1:Wait/Sleep,2:Wait/Sema`, `loader_waits_module=44/212` frozen since b300, `stream_rpc_calls` +2/boundary = the cmd-9 status polls) because the EE has not asked for anything else yet. *(5) Projection:* at one read per boundary the ≈4,700-read Beach load completes near **b≈4,900** (OLD b852, vf 1374; OLD's own DVD model put the retail pacing of this load at "5581 reads => 14.8s", `hle_heap.cpp:2735-2737`, i.e. ≈6 completions per frame on hardware — the console observes completions mid-frame at the title's poll, as OLD does, only slower than OLD's 23). *Checked, not the cause:* `fmv_abort_holds=2` (released; the FMV pictures 547/547 served), `iop_bus_window_hits=0`, `cdvd_latency_model=instant`, `ticks=cdvd=0`. The W142 lane: `diff_cb900.out` `audio.ahash: FIRST DIVERGENT audio window at line 143 (key 142): A: 142,0x6e5c320f7c150766 B: 142,0x0a6fb8af536edb25` (NEW still silent, as expected without a change); `mem.mhash` line 60 `0x2c5b6d50e6b67abf` (= I8.3-G). **Class:** TIMING DOMAIN — sub-tick (in-slice) visibility of a completed loader read at the title's own poll; not a construction gap, not OLD-wrong (OLD's in-frame pump is a wall-coupled host hook, BUG-026, but hardware DOES complete reads mid-frame), not fixable under the stage's rules (MUST-STOP: "how … sub-tick behave"). **Options.** (a) an in-slice delivery seam at the engaged load-wait site: when `ShouldYieldPollSlice` would fire for an in-flight read whose MODELLED due time (`DeliveryMeter::DueTick`/busy-time in tick units, `cdvd.latency_model=modelled`, deterministic — a function of lba/sectors and the tick domain, never host time) falls inside the current slice, deliver and release it at the poll instead of lifting (counter `loader_inslice_deliveries=`, guards as the lift's, pin: a chained read sequence completes N-per-boundary by the model; HOOK_RECLASSIFICATION row); this changes `mem.mhash` from the first in-slice delivery (b60 class re-run, DP-4 rows re-checked) and every anchor restored mid-load; (b) keep boundary-only delivery and move the coldboot gate's title-screen leg to ≈6,000 frames (≈10 min at 0.1 s/frame; the §4.4 stopping rule stays at W28); (c) a bounded "k deliveries per boundary" budget (no model; the fastest to land, but a constant of ours, not the console's). **Recommendation.** (a) — it is the only option that models what the hardware does (completion visible when the drive is done, at the poll that asks), and the plan already classes the sync-wait lift at this site. No decision.

## DP-18 — f57292 (the FMV-entry anchor) never reaches 600 frames: before the pad fix it parked `fatal_no_jump` (`pc=0x0 tid=1`) at frame 190 with 124 unhandled MMIO pages; after BUG-065's fix it stalls at frame 15 inside the title's `FUN_0027edf0` — the unhandled MMIO is a guest pointer walking the register window, not an unrouted device (2026-10-02, I8.6-A)

**Evidence (`int_work/i86a/`: `f57292_4/run.meta`, `f57292_200/` + `f57292_200_wrap.log`, `sample_f57292_stall.txt`, `sample_f57292_200.txt`; I8.5-G `battery/ps2state_slot0_f57292/run.meta`):** (1) The 124 pages of I8.5-G's `unhandled_mmio_pages` are a CONTIGUOUS sweep — `0x10002000…0x10002f00`, `0x10003100…0x10003700`, `0x10003900…0x10003b00`, `0x10003d00…0x10003f00`, `0x10004000…0x10007f00`, `0x1000e000…0x1000f100`, `0x1000f300…0x1000ff00` — i.e. every 256-byte page of `0x10002000–0x10007fff` and `0x1000e000–0x1000ffff` except the pages a device claims (GIF `0x10003000`, VIF0 `0x10003800`, VIF1 `0x10003c00`, SIF `0x1000f200`). No PS2 device decodes `0x10004000–0x10007fff` (FIFOs are 16 bytes at `0x10004000/5000/6000/7000`) or `0x1000e000+0x100…`: this is a guest data pointer (base ≈ `0x10000000`) being walked through the window, not a register the title programs. The new run.meta census (`MmioRouter::FirstUnhandledAccess`, key `unhandled_mmio_first=`, I8.6-A) names the first such access on a 4-frame leg: **`unhandled_mmio_first=r2@0x10003418`** — a 16-bit read of an unmapped word (`0x10003400` page), already inside the first 4 boundaries (the leg finalizes: `frames=4 finalized=1 park=none`). (2) With BUG-065 fixed the title leaves the controller-disconnected hold it sat in during I8.5-G's 190 frames and, at frame 15, enters **`FUN_0027edf0`** (1,284 bytes, 25 blocks, four 116-byte loop bodies at `0x27ef8c/0x27f000/0x27f0e4/0x27f1a4`; allocations through `FUN_001816c8` and `FUN_00205528` at entry) called from `FUN_00282098 ← FUN_002927f0 ← FUN_00248d80 ← FUN_00242c58 ← FUN_001ed448 ← FUN_001ec350 ← FUN_0017d47c ← FUN_0017ce50` (the scene walk) and never leaves it: `sample` of the guest thread (3 s, 2,536 samples) = 100 % `FUN_0027edf0` → `AddressMap::Translate`/`RecompAbi::Memory` (a memory loop); the other guest thread sleeps in `FUN_001e7f80 → SceSif::RpcLoop → ThreadManager::SleepThread`. `frames.fhash` stops at 15 lines (`57293…57306`); `run_bounded` kills the leg on its stall bound (`no growth … for 60s`), `finalized=0`. The pre-fix leg (I8.5-G) reached `pc=0x0` only because the hold kept the title out of this path for 190 frames; the `jr` to 0 is downstream of the same walk. (3) OLD's baseline leg for f57292 reaches 600 frames in 21.7 s (`verify/baseline/ps2state_slot0_f57292/run.meta`), so the walk terminates on OLD: either OLD's MMIO window returns values that end the loop (OLD `memory.cpp:539-981` idle table — NEW's `MmioSink` reproduces it for the DOCUMENTED registers; `0x10003418`/`0x10004100…` have no row on either side and read 0 on NEW), or the pointer OLD walks is a RAM address (an allocation that differs: f57292 is the FMV-entry anchor; the allocators `FUN_001816c8`/`FUN_00205528` at `FUN_0027edf0`'s entry are the first suspects — DP-8 (2)'s heap shift is the open item that moves allocations between OLD and NEW).

(4) Gate run 3 (`int_work/i86a/battery.log`): f57292 `frames.fhash` 15 lines then killed (`run.meta not finalized`); **f59991 now stalls too** — 126 `frames.fhash` lines (≈ frame 125) then no growth for 60 s (it reached 600 in I8.5-G while the title sat in the disconnect hold); not sampled this stage — same class suspected (a scene-walk path the hold previously masked), to be confirmed by option (a)/(b) on f59991 as well.

**Why not decided here:** not a construction gap in the pre-authorized sense — no device owns `0x10003418` or `0x10004100–0x10007fff`, so there is nothing to route; "model the register" would invent values for addresses the hardware does not decode; and the OLD-side value of the walked pointer is not in evidence (no OLD f57292 images exist — only the baseline lanes).

**Options:** (a) **Produce the OLD evidence first:** an OLD f57292 leg with `PS2_VERIFY_STATE_EVERY=14` (`old_art.py` shape; ≈2 s, 44 MB) → OLD `state_b13` (= boundary 14) vs NEW `state_b10`/`b14`, then `word_diff` on the arguments `FUN_0027edf0` receives (its `$a0/$a1` are saved at `sp+0x80/+0xb0` on entry; the recompiled prologue in `recompiled_shard_026.cpp`) — attributes the pointer. (b) **Attribute on NEW alone:** a bounded leg with the EE ring channel on (`--set` the verbose tier) and `ring_decode` of `logs/ring_crash.rlog` after a `--frames 20` kill, or a `MmioRouter` first-access census extended with the guest `pc`/`tid` (needs the access site to pass the ctx — an ABI-adjacent change; MUST-STOP class if it touches `engine/ee/recomp_abi`). (c) **Defer:** keep f57292 as the one anchor that does not finalize, record it in the §4.5 verdict table, and gate on the other ten until DP-8 (2) (heap shift) is attributed — if the walked pointer is an allocation, DP-8 (2)'s fix closes this too.

**Recommendation:** (a) then (b)-ring: cheap OLD evidence (bounded, read-only), no code until the pointer's provenance is known. No §7 row, no mask.

The sweep `iop_func_0000a2ac` then kills type-1 voices whose ENDX bit is latched (`SdSetParam(ADSR2=0x1fc0)` + `SdSetSwitch(KOFF, mask)` + ENDX clear) — the module's SFX voice lifecycle. *OLD:* `oldmusq/slowspot_f40298….log` `[KSWQ] vf=40304 KOFF core=0 mask=0xfffffc ra=0xa3c0` and `vf=40305 KOFF core=1 mask=0xffffb2` — the masks equal the anchor's restored ENDX latches (`anchors_sndc.out` `endx=0xfffffc,0xffffb2`); OLD `state_b1` IOP RAM type bytes `0x12f60..` read `01` (released) or `00` (freed) — `type0=4, type1=44`, no `04` left — countdown words `0x13050..` all 0, SNDC voices v02–v23/v25/v28/v29/v31–v47 `phase Off, adsr2 0x1fc0` (`pair_b1_b5.out`). *NEW:* the same images keep type bytes `04` (`type1=6, type4=42`, unchanged from the anchor), words `0xffffffff`, voices `Sustain, adsr2 0x1fcd`, and the module never issues `SdSetSwitch(ENDX, 0)` (NEW b1/b5 ENDX core1 reads `0xffff92` vs the anchor's `0xffffb2`: one bit, v29, moved in the voice model; OLD's reads 0 after the sweep's clear) — and under lldb over 10 frames (`lldb_leg.log`, W0 hash unchanged `0x62b6edcca4681814`): `iop_func_0000136c … hit count = 0`, `iop_func_0000a1e0 … hit count = 0`, while `iop_func_0000a2ac … 12` and the RPC-side `iop_func_00004394 … 12` (the worker is woken per frame by the EE's RPC, never by the heartbeat) although `run.meta iop_timer_running=1` (`StreamIrxPlugin::RestoreHeartbeat` → `TimrmanModule::RestoreHardTimer` armed it). Writer: `IopmSection::ReconcileKernel` (`engine/iop/savestate/iopm_section.cpp:120-122`, "FRAME clock rebase: forward-only to the restored anchor's frame count", `kernel.Clock().AdvanceToUs(restored_vf * IopClock::kFrameUs)`) moves `virtual_us_` to the anchor's frame count while `IopClock::frames_` stays 0; `IopClock::OnFrameBoundary` (`engine/iop/iop_clock.cpp:11-19`: `frames_ += 1; AdvanceToUs(frames_ * kFrameUs)`, and `AdvanceToUs` only moves forwards) therefore cannot raise `virtual_us_` for ~40304 frames, so the restored timer's `due_us = NowUs() + 16727` (`iop_kernel.cpp:429`; fired only when `due_us <= now`, `:535`) is unreachable in any leg (events due "at once" still fire, which is why transfers and RPC completions work). **Proposed fix (construction stage, pre-authorized):** make the restored heartbeat beat on the boundary clock after a restore (re-base the kernel clock's frame count to `virtual_us_` at re-park, or arm the restored timer's first due at the next boundary tick), with a unit pin (`TimrmanModule::RestoreHardTimer` + `IopKernel` clock: a beat fires within one frame quantum after a restore that follows a forced advance) and a root pin (an anchor leg `--frames 4 --state-every 1`: IOP type bytes `0x12f60..` of the finished voices read 1 then 0 and the sweep's KOFF count > 0; `run.meta` candidate `iop_hb_beats=`). Not OLD-wrong; not timing-domain (DP-8) — the beat is the module's own vsync-period timer, which OLD fires per frame.

OLD's `IopClock` (`iop_kernel/iop_clock.h:43-65`) has NO frames rebase — `OnFrameBoundary` raises `virtual_us_` only to `(frames_+1) × 16667` and `FrameCeilingUs()` is `(frames_+1) × 16667`, so after a restore to vf ≈ 40304 (virtual 671,746,768 µs) with `frames_` still at its boot count, every boundary pump (`iop_module_table.cpp:88-100` `DrainQuantum`) is ONE `Dispatch()` then `break` (next due > ceiling) and the clock only moves by the RPC drive (`sif_rpc_bridge.cpp:57-75` `DriveUntil`: `AdvanceToUs(*r.next_due_us)` while the EE's RPC is incomplete) and the park-loop pump — i.e. OLD carries exactly the BUG-066 defect NEW fixed. *Phase:* OLD's heartbeat was armed at module boot (`[IOP-STUB] StartHardTimer(timer=1): period 16727 us … (iop-kernel timer)`, beat `[IOPSCHED] vf=1 hb_beat 0 0 t=16727`) with a boot-time due; the rebase makes that stale due ≤ now, so the FIRST Dispatch after the load beats: `[E2-SAVE] LOADED … (vf=40304 …)` → `[IOPSCHED] vf=40304 hb_beat 0 0 t=671746768` → `signal_sema 0 0` (the handler's `iSignalSema`) → `signal_sema 0 1` + `[MUSQ7] vf=40304 cmd=9` (the EE's RPC) → `wait_sema_woke 0 2` → `[ENVXQ]` polls (the release pass) → `[KSWQ] vf=40304 KOFF core=0 mask=0xfffffc ra=0xa3c0` (the sweep, mask = the restored ENDX latch) → `vf=40305 KOFF core=1 mask=0xffffb2`. So OLD's restored timer is **"due at once"** — not a saved remaining time (neither side saves the timer: OLD `iop_stubs.cpp:2811-3030` IOPM has no timer word, NEW's IOPM layout none — the module's descriptor words only), not boot-symmetric — an artefact of the stale due. NEW (`lldb_new6.log`): `TIMER_START period_us=16727 clock.virtual_us=671746768 clock.frames=40304` (due = now + 16727, `iop_kernel.cpp:429`), then in boundary 0 `HIT sweep_a2ac`, `HIT rpc_4394` (the RPC-woken sweep clears ENDX) BEFORE `CLK_BOUNDARY frames=40304 virtual_us=671746768`, and only then `HIT handler_136c`, `HIT release_a1e0`, `HIT sweep_a2ac` (boundary 1's pump; `FrameCeilingUs` inclusive reaches the due 60 µs past the boundary) — the I8.8-A W0 residual mechanism, confirmed. *Cadence:* OLD beats ONCE after the restore and never again — f40298 6 frames: `hb_beat` lines = 2 (vf 1 and vf 40304), the IOP clock's last `t=` values `671746768, 671746808, 671746848` (+80 µs over 5 boundaries: two 40 µs `DelayThread` expiries driven by the RPC); artdet 14 frames: `hb_beat` at vf 1 and `vf=37156 t=619279052` only, clock `619279052 → 619279692` (+640 µs over 13 boundaries; next due 619,295,779 never reached). NEW: one beat per boundary (`hit count = 6` each for `0000136c`/`0000a1e0` over 6 frames; gate run 4 `iop_hb_beats=598/600` = 600 × 16667/16727 = 597.8 — the retail period). *Hardware:* the module arms `SetTimerHandler(timer=1, compare=616642 …)` at 36.864 MHz → 616642/36.864 = 16727.1 µs (59.78 Hz), free-running regardless of EE RPCs — NEW runs the retail period; OLD's post-restore heartbeat is dead (its one beat is the rebase artefact). **Class: OLD-wrong (cadence) — a MUST-STOP accepted-difference candidate → DP-20; the PHASE (first due at once vs one period later) is a savestate-format gap on both sides (the timer's remaining time is not saved) → also DP-20, with a modelled-rule recommendation.**


## DP-20 — The anchors' audio residual after BUG-066 is OLD's dead post-restore heartbeat (OLD-wrong cadence) plus an unsaved timer PHASE both sides guess: OLD beats once at the restore (a stale boot due expired by its clock rebase) and never again; NEW beats the retail 16727 µs period but its first due is one period after the restore, so the RPC-woken sweep precedes the first release pass (2026-10-02, I8.9-B)

**Evidence (`int_work/i89b/`; the I8.9-B attribution paragraph under DP-8 (1)'s heartbeat status quotes the lines):** OLD `host_app/iop_stubs.cpp:3006-3017` rebases only `virtual_us_` at the IOPM restore (`k.Clock().AdvanceToUs(g_vsync_frame × kFrameUs)`), its `IopClock` (`iop_kernel/iop_clock.h:43-65`) never rebases `frames_`, so `OnFrameBoundary` and `FrameCeilingUs()` stay ≈ boot values and the boundary pump is a single `Dispatch()` per boundary; the heartbeat's stale boot due fires at the first Dispatch (`[IOPSCHED] vf=40304 hb_beat 0 0 t=671746768`, before the worker's `[KSWQ] vf=40304 KOFF core=0 mask=0xfffffc`) and its re-armed due `now + 16727` is never reached (clock +80 µs over 5 boundaries on f40298, +640 µs over 13 on artdet; `hb_beat` count after the restore = 1 on both legs). NEW (`lldb_new6.log`): `TIMER_START period_us=16727 clock.virtual_us=671746768 clock.frames=40304`; boundary 0 = `sweep_a2ac`, `rpc_4394` only; from boundary 1 `handler_136c → release_a1e0 → sweep_a2ac` every boundary (`hit count = 6` over 6 frames; `iop_hb_beats=598/600` on the gate). Hardware period: `compare=616642` / 36.864 MHz = 16727 µs, free-running (the module's `SetupHardTimer`/`StartHardTimer` at init; `iop_recompiled.cpp` `iop_func_000012c0`/`0000136c`). Consequences measured by I8.8-A: with NEW's retail cadence the module frees released records within a beat while OLD's images keep type-4 records ≥ 5 boundaries after a KON → artdet W1, slowspot_f37092 W5, f44111 W1, f46949 W1 (the cadence class); with NEW's one-period first due the boundary-0 RPC-woken sweep issues `SdSetSwitch(ENDX, 0)` before the first release pass → f36935 v24, f39807 v25/v28, slowspot_f40298 v29 are not keyed off in pump 0/1 (the phase class, W0 on three anchors).

**Why a decision:** (1) the cadence is an OLD-wrong claim (OLD's post-restore IOP clock is frozen — the very defect BUG-066 fixed in NEW; OLD's silence of released voices on the anchors is an artefact, the retail module beats every 16.7 ms) → an accepted-difference row (§7) for the anchors' audio lane from the first beat on, with no mask (audio hashes have none); (2) the first-due phase is not recorded by either tree (no timer word in the IOPM section on either side; the module's descriptor carries id/compare only), so both sides guess: OLD "at once", NEW "one period later" — a modelled rule must be chosen, and carrying the remaining time would be a savestate-format change (breaks the 11 frozen anchors) — MUST-STOP.

**Options:** (a) **Cadence: accept** — row `P5-ANCHORS-OLD-HEARTBEAT-DEAD-AFTER-RESTORE` (audio lane; OLD's post-restore release pass does not run; NEW runs the retail period; evidence above; no mask) and re-baseline the anchors' audio windows from NEW's own first-beat behaviour (the W1/W5 anchors cannot match OLD without reproducing OLD's defect). (b) **Cadence: reproduce OLD** (a seam that stops the restored heartbeat after one beat) — rejected by the stage: it is the BUG-066 defect re-introduced; stated for completeness. (c) **Phase: model "a restored running timer is due at the restore"** (first beat in the FIRST pump of boundary 0, before the EE's RPC can wake the worker): `TimrmanModule::RestoreHardTimer`/`IopKernel::StartPeriodicTimerUs` variant with `due = NowUs()` (or `RestoreHeartbeat` arming at the rebased now minus the period) — matches OLD's observable order (beat → release → sweep → KOFF with the full restored ENDX mask), costs no format change, closes the three W0 anchors' v24/v25/v28/v29 residual (the released voices are keyed off in pump 0/1 as on OLD); pins: unit (`TimrmanModule::RestoreHardTimer` beats in the first `Dispatch` after the rebase) + root (`RootAnchorHeartbeat` asserts `state_b1 v29 Off`, re-enabling I8.8-A's dropped check). (d) **Phase: keep NEW's one-period rule** and accept the three anchors' W0 as a §7 row (the kill one pump later than OLD) — a smaller model change but a permanent W0 on three anchors with no hardware argument for either guess. (e) **Phase: save the remaining time** — a new savestate section/word — format change, breaks the frozen anchors; rejected unless the user wants a v5 image format.

**Recommendation:** (a) + (c). (a) because OLD's cadence is the frozen-clock defect, not a rule; (c) because "due at the restore" is the only phase choice that reproduces OLD's W0 behaviour on every anchor measured (I8.7-B `[KSWQ]` masks = the restored ENDX latches) without a format change, and because a timer that was mid-period at the save has, on hardware, at most one period left — firing at the restore is within that bound (the earliest admissible phase), whereas NEW's rule is the latest. After (c), gate run 5 should show f36935/f39807/slowspot_f40298 W0 → identical through the window where OLD's one beat and NEW's first beat coincide, with the remaining audio differences starting at the FIRST boundary OLD's missing second beat would have mattered (the (a) row's onset per anchor — to be named by the gate).

**User decision:**


## DP-21 — The anchors' 1–2 ULP float words at `0x585410/0x585418/0x585420` are NOT an EE FPU (COP1) op: the writers are `sq`/`sqc2` copies of a 4×4 matrix produced by the VU0 MICRO program `0x790` (the libvu0-style 4-row concat `vmulax/vmadday/vmaddaz/vmaddw`) fed by `FUN_00294300`'s quaternion blends `0x548/0x668`; every FP operator class on that chain has the SAME semantics in both trees (fused MADD, identical FDIV bodies, chop rounding), so the operands must already differ — the op cannot be adjudicated without an operand capture on both sides (2026-10-02, I8.11-B)

**Evidence (`int_work/i811b/`; evidence only, no build, no leg).** *Writers → source lines:* the diag binary's DWARF maps I8.8-B's `art62/watch.log` sites (shard sources unchanged since `747dff2`, so the host offsets hold): `atos` → `FUN_00294b18+0xec0 = recompiled_shard_027.cpp:24850`, `+0xee8 = :24854`, `FUN_00296228+0x2908 = :33385`, `+0x291c = :33386`, `FUN_0018e620+0x9d8 = recompiled_shard_009.cpp:16694`, `FUN_00192270+0x9b0 = :30022`. Those lines are **128-bit stores observed as two 8-byte halves** (the WordWatch rule), not arithmetic: `FUN_00294b18` block `0x294bc4` — `// sq instruction - 128-bit store` `memory::write_quad(ctx.cpuRegs.GPR.r[4].UL[0] + 0x20, …r[3])` / `+ 0x30, …r[2]` after `lq` from `[s1+0x20/0x30]` (the matrix rows 2–3 copied from the result at `s1` into the arena record `a0 = [s3+0xc]`; `0x585410` = record `+0x20`, `0x585420` = `+0x30`); `FUN_00296228` block `0x296424` — `// sqc2 - Store Quadword from COP2 (VU0 VF)` `memory::write_quad(r[6] + 0x20, …VF[3])` / `+ 0x30, …VF[4]` right after `memcpy(&VF[1..4], &r[15],r[13],r[10],r[8])` (local pose rows), `memcpy(&VF[5..8], &r[14],r[11],r[9],r[7])` (parent rows), `g_vu0.Execute(ctx, 0x790)` (block `0x2963b0`); `FUN_0018e620`/`FUN_00192270` likewise `sqc2 VF28/29/30` and `lq/sq` copies. The generated MIPS for `FUN_00294b18`'s own body holds no COP1 arithmetic at all (one `mtc1 1.0f`, loads/stores, two `recomp_call`s to `FUN_00294480`/`FUN_00294300` and one vtable `jalr`). *The producer:* `games/…/vu1_library/vu0_programs.cpp:21` `{0, 0x0790, "VU0 4x4 matrix concat (pose compose / streaming)", Kind::Unimplemented, 0x01e329bc8000033c, 0x01e330bd8000033c}` = OLD `vu_function_registry.cpp:76-84` ("qmtc2 parent rows -> vf05-08, local pose in vf01-04, vcallms 0x790; the micro is a 4-row matrix concat (vmulax/vmadday/vmaddaz/vmaddw per row, E-bit at 0x808)", PCSX2 micro-mem fingerprint `3c 03 00 80 bc 29 e3 01 | 3c 03 00 80 bd 30 e3 01`); both trees run it on their VU interpreter (NEW `engine/vu/tiers/vu_interpreter_tier*.cpp` / `vu_cached_tier_exec.cpp`; OLD `host_app/vu.cpp` interp/cached arms). The value that differs is the SECOND writer's (`FUN_00296228`): NEW `0x585410` `0xbf75cd233e8e1e63 -> 0x3f7c7917be28594e` vs OLD boundary 2 `…be28594c` (row 2 `.y`, 2 ULP; row 3 words 1 ULP). *Operator semantics compared, class by class (no gap found):* (1) MADD family — NEW `inline float Fma(float a, float b, float c) { return std::fmaf(a, b, c); }` (`vu_interpreter_tier_upper.cpp:25`, `vu_cached_tier_exec.cpp:26`: "single-rounding fused multiply-add"); OLD `result.x = ctx.vuRegs.ACC.x + (src1.x * bc_val)` (`vu.cpp:8203`, MADDA `acc_c.x + s1.x * bc_val` `:8795`) with NO `-ffp-contract` flag — **the host toolchain (Apple clang 21.0.0) fuses that shape by default at every level**: `int_work/i811b/contract_probe.cpp` (acc=3, s=bc=0x3f800001, nearest) → `-O0/-O2/-O3 default: expr=40800001 fma=40800001 split=40800000 -> expr is FUSED` (`fmadd s0, s1, s2, s0` in the disassembly), `-O2 contract=off: expr=40800000 … UNFUSED` → both trees' VU MADD are fused; (2) FDIV pipe — `DoDiv` `q = num / den`, `DoRsqrt` `q = num / std::sqrt(std::fabs(d))` (NEW `vu_interpreter_tier_upper.cpp:284-326`) ≡ OLD `qv = num / den` / `qv = num / std::sqrt(std::fabs(den_sq))` (`vu.cpp:9300-9420`), same clamp and by-zero rules; (3) rounding — NEW `VuChopRoundGuard` (`vu_interpreter_tier.cpp:68`, `vu_cached_tier.cpp:112`: FMAC/FDIV/EFU truncate) and the guest thread's `FE_TOWARDZERO` (P4-EE-ROUNDING) ≡ OLD's `fesetround(FE_TOWARDZERO)` on every recompiled thread; (4) the macro-mode ops in `FUN_00294300` (`vsub`, `vaddw`, `vmaxw`, `vmove`) and the emitted macro MADD shape (`ctx.vuRegs.ACC + (fs * VR_reg{…})` through `VR_reg::operator+/operator*` — OLD `recompiler.cpp:23597-23607`, NEW shard `recompiled_shard_009.cpp` `// vmaddw` block) are the same text on both sides and UNFUSED on both (separate operator calls; NEW shards also `-ffp-contract=off`, P4-FPCONTRACT). *Already accepted rows:* P4-CKPT0-LIBM-ULP / P4-EE-ROUNDING (PHASE4_PLAN §7.1) cover the EE COP1 rounding-mode class, CLOSED as "NEW was wrong, fixed" — this class is NOT that one (no COP1 op on the chain; the EE rounding fix is in place in the leg's build). No Ps2FloatRounding row covers a VU0 class.

**Why a decision:** the prompt's three outcomes (NEW `engine/ee/fpu` gap / OLD wrong / VU0 op) resolve to the third, but with identical operator semantics on both sides the 1–2 ULP must enter through the OPERANDS (the local-pose rows from the quaternion blends `0x548`/`0x668`, whose Q-pipe timing — `WAITQ`/`kDivDelay`/`kRsqrtDelay` retirement against the E-bit — differs in model between the trees' flag pipelines, or the parent rows from an earlier record already 1 ULP off), and neither tree records VF01–08 at the `vcallms`. Naming the op needs an operand capture on BOTH sides; the PS2 rule for the VU's MADD rounding itself is an open hardware question (PCSX2's interpreter evaluates the FMAC through `vuDouble()` promotions — OLD `vu.cpp:1124` cites `VUops.cpp _vuDIV:954 VU->q.F = vuDouble(VU->q.UL)` — i.e. in double with one conversion, neither "fused float" nor "two float roundings"; its microVU recompiler uses SSE `mulps`+`addps` = two roundings; ps2tek does not state the FMAC's internal precision — none of this was re-read from PCSX2 sources in this stage) — any "OLD is wrong"/"NEW is wrong" claim here would be a §7 row → MUST-STOP.

**Options:** (a) **Operand capture, both sides (recommended next evidence):** OLD already carries `[VCALLMS-PROBE]` (`host_app/vu.cpp:5870-5920`: the first 24 `vcallms 0x548/0x668/0x790` after `g_vsync_frame >= 1150`, `in vf01..08` hex + out `vf01..04`; needs `PS2_LOG=1`, log-only, bounded — it did not fire in I8.8-B's `old_f57292_3` log, so its gate must be re-read before relying on it) — add the NEW diag-only equivalent (a `Vu0Registry` census hook: `NoteEntry` already exists, PHASE5_IMPL 5D HANDOFF) that dumps VF01–08 in / VF01–04 out per `vcallms` for a `--watch-word`-style address filter, run artdet `--frames 3` on both, and diff the FIRST call whose output differs: operands equal → the op (then adjudicate per (b)); operands differ → walk back (the Q pipe / the parent record). (b) **Hardware arbiter:** a PCSX2 run of the same anchor with the EE/VU in the default Chop mode and a memory read of `0x585410` after boundary 2 (`pcsx2-debug read_memory`) names the console's side without resolving the op. (c) **Accept as a §7 row** `P5-VU0-CONCAT-ULP` (mem lane, the pose-matrix records; ≤ 2 ULP; no mask) — premature: no attribution to an operator yet.

**Recommendation:** (a) then (b); no row, no mask, no fix (there is no construction gap to fix: every operator on the chain is modelled identically on both trees, and the only standing requirement — chop rounding on every VU/EE thread — is verified in place).
