**DP-22 — DECISION (MADE 2026-10-03 ≈08:16–08:21, P8.1-A — the first record said 08:55, a time after the stage's own end at 08:32 (review MINOR 2); from the title's code + the SIF/cdvdman contract, not from a leg).** *The title's contract, read from the recompiled shards (`FUN_001f40e0`, `FUN_001f5428`, `FUN_001f54a0`, `FUN_002b6050`, `FUN_001f42b0`, `FUN_001ece48`, `FUN_002b4738`, `FUN_002ab7a8`, `FUN_002b6018`, `FUN_001f55c0`, `FUN_001f1b10`; vtables read from the ELF: listener `0x2fbdb0` slot `+0x44 = FUN_001f55c0` (notify), slot `+0x64 = FUN_001f42b0` (stage step); notification record vtable `0x306f60`):* `FUN_001f40e0(level, ·, ·)` runs ONLY when the level's node list `*(level+0x1c)` is empty (`0x1f4104–0x1f4114`: `p = *(level+0x1c); if (p && *p) return 0`); then it creates ONE node programmatically (`new(0x1f0)` → ctor `FUN_001ec6a0`, `+0x144 = 0`), links it (`FUN_001012f8(level+0x1c, node)`: `*(level+0x1c) = cell`, `*cell = node` — the frame-302 writers `0xc1aa7c = 0xbb62b0`, `0xbf8b60+0 = 0xbb62b0` of the watch legs), creates the request listener (`FUN_001f5428`: `0xb97780`, `+0x14 = level`) and ISSUES THE LEVEL LOAD (`FUN_001f54a0` → `FUN_002b6050(listener, name, 0)`: notification record `{vtbl 0x306f60, +4 = listener}` + a job of type `0x28` enqueued on the loader SM by `FUN_002b68f0`). The STAGE step `FUN_001f42b0` (P8.2-A: it is slot `+0x14` of the per-entry record vtable `0x2fbe00` built by `FUN_001f5540` — `0x2fbe14`, the word P8.1-A read as listener `0x2fbdb0+0x64`; the record's `+0xc` is the listener and `+4` the entry type that selects the state; it runs from the sub-resource read job's notify `FUN_002abec8`), state 0 (`0x1f4320–0x1f4390`): opens the level stream (`FUN_002ac428`, `FUN_002ab740`) and DESERIALISES INTO THE LIST HEAD NODE — `FUN_001ece48(**(level+0x1c), stream)` — which, for stream tag `0x160a`, creates the node's listener (`new(0x90)`, vtable `0x2fb520`, `sw s0,0x144(s2)` at `0x1ecf9c`); then re-enqueues the job. The loader SM (`FUN_002b4738`, mode 0) dispatches the job's NOTIFY (`vtbl+0x24` → `FUN_002ab7a8` → `FUN_002b6018` → listener `+0x44 = FUN_001f55c0`) only after the job's `isDone` (`vtbl+0x1c`) answers true; `FUN_001f55c0` then calls the head node's listener through `FUN_001f1b10` UNCONDITIONALLY. **The invariant the title's code assumes: a load job's stage steps (state 0's deserialise, which fills `+0x144`) run to completion BEFORE its completion notify is dispatched — i.e. the notify observes a job whose reads have all landed and been processed.** On the console this holds because cdvdman completes the job's reads in issue order and the job's `isDone` sees "done" only after the data the stage consumed has landed. *What NEW did at frame 309 (`int_work/p81a/w1/`, diag binary, `--watch-word 0xc1aa7c,0xb97794,0xbf8ca4,0xb97798,0xb9779c`, `frames=309 park=fatal_no_jump`):* `0xc1aa7c` written at b302 by `FUN_001012f8` (`0x0->0xbb62b0`), `0xbf8ca4` once (`FUN_001ec6a0`, 0), nothing else to b309 — the stage-0 deserialise never touched the node (its first stores, `+0x120` via the stream's read method, never fired: the w309c watch of `0xbf8c80` shows only the ctor and `FUN_001f13d0`); and the loader trace ends `1491 3783 enq fs=0xbb3560 src=ee lba=522998 sec=1 end=24963.422` then `yld … blk=inflt` to tick 1496 with NO `dlv` for that read in any poll leg (`i815a/poll1200f`, `i816a/poll320`, `p81a/w1`: 907 `dlv` for 908 `enq`) — the notify at tick 1497 (frame 309) was dispatched while the job's own 1-sector header read (lba 522998 = the level file's first sector; a long seek from the head) was STILL IN FLIGHT on the modelled drive. **That is the ordering NEW violated: a job's completion was observed before its last read completed** — the SIF/cdvdman rule "a completion is observed only after the drive delivered the read, and reads are observed in issue order" (one drive; `sceCdSync`/the status word answer busy until the drive is idle). [CORRECTED by P8.2-A after the P8.1-A review (MAJOR 1): the job DISPATCHED at frame 309 is not a read job — it is the "all sub-resources done" CALLBACK job (secondary vtable `0x3067d8`, ctor `FUN_002ab740`, `isDone = FUN_002ab7a0 ≡ 1`, `+0x24 = FUN_002ab7a8` → record `0x306b00` `+0x14 = FUN_002b6018` → listener `+0x44 = FUN_001f55c0`), which `FUN_002b3f18` enqueues AFTER its `r19` sub-resource read jobs; it owns no read and evaluates no fs predicate. The loader SM (`FUN_002b4738`) holds ONE current job (`sm+0x18`), polls only ITS `isDone`, and on done notifies, destroys and dequeues the next from a FIFO ring (`FUN_002b4618`; jobs enqueued inside a notify go to a deferred list that is pushed to the FRONT in order). So the callback job reaches dispatch only after every job queued ahead of it answered done — the QUEUE ORDER is the invariant, and NEW did not reorder the queue: it let the HEADER READ job (record `0x306f60`, the job of `FUN_002b6050`) answer done at frame 306 with its 1-sector read undelivered (`p82a/w2/watch.log`: `b306 FUN_002ad110+0x161c 0xbb3560 0x30300->0x20300`), so `FUN_002b40f0` parsed a header of count 0 → the callback job directly (no entry-table job, `r19 = 0`, no stage-0 deserialise) → the notify on frame 309. The NEW path (named by the `w2` leg): the HLE marks the STREAM slot byte `0x3b3d00+slot` "issued" (2) at the async issue; the title's own reply processor `FUN_001e1f48` ages a slot the module's status replies never list — `2 → 5` on the first reply (`b302`), then the per-slot countdown `0x3b3d30+slot` `3 → 2 → 1 → 0` (`b303..b305`) and the byte `5 → 0` = done (`b305 FUN_001e1f48+0x51a4`) — because the recompiled STREAM.IRX knows nothing of a host-served record; on the console the module lists the slot in every reply while the IOP reads it (the byte reads 1, active, and never ages). The earlier candidates (an untracked slot, the SM mode word, an `isDone` that ignores the read) are excluded by the same leg.] **Decision:** (1) the DECIDED ordering: completions are visible in ISSUE ORDER at every observation point — the title's poll (the meter's `CompleteNow(tick)` drains the queue head-first; already so), slot 4 (`DeliverDue` head-first; already so) and the BLOCKING WAIT, which until now delivered only the waited read and left earlier-issued reads on other objects "in flight" after a later one had landed (an inversion the console cannot show): `LoaderModel::CompleteNow(fs)` now first delivers every read queued ahead of the waited one (`DeliveryMeter::CompleteBefore(id)`; census `cdvd_wait_order_deliveries`, trace `dlv src=wait-order`), under `cdvd.delivery_visibility=poll` only (boundary mode: `CompleteBefore` returns 0 — bit-identical by construction). Pin: `LoaderModelAndDeliveryBudget` `TestPollOrderAtEveryPoint` (a wait on the later of two reads lands the earlier first; three contiguous reads land head-first at the poll; boundary mode unchanged) — the toggle is the existing visibility key. (2) `FUN_001f1b10` stays as written (the title's code does not test `+0x144`; option (b) rejected as before). (3) The frame-309 completion itself is NOT in-order-explained by (1) (the waited reads of the burst are on one object each) — it is the "header read job done before its read landed" class above (corrected: the premature completion is the HEADER job's at frame 306; the frame-309 dispatch is its consequence, the callback job), carried as BUG-069's root cause, fixed and pinned in P8.2-A (the attempt-3 paragraph below); the title-level pin (`RootCdvdPollVisibility` ≥ 400 frames, no park) was NOT extended in P8.1-A (it would have been red). Attempt count for DP-22: 2 of 3.

**DP-22 — attempt 3 of 3 (P8.2-A, 2026-10-03 09:06–10:05; `int_work/p82a/`; the NEW gap NAMED from a leg, FIXED behind the existing toggle, PINNED; default still `boundary`, `boundary` bit-identical: `p82a/bnd200` vs `fmvfix/det200` `frames.fhash IDENTICAL (200 lines) mem.mhash IDENTICAL (4) drawir.ohash IDENTICAL (200) gsops.ohash IDENTICAL (200) audio.ahash IDENTICAL (34)`).** *Static (the queue order, from the shards + ELF vtables):* the loader SM `FUN_002b4738` holds ONE current job (`sm+0x18`) and polls only ITS `isDone` (`vtbl+0x1c`); on done it sets `sm+0 |= 8`, notifies (`+0x24`), destroys (`+0xc`), clears the slot and dequeues the next through `FUN_002b4618` — a FIFO ring (`sm+0x8` buffer, `+0x10` head, `+0x14` count), preceded by the deferred list (`sm+0x1c`, jobs `FUN_002b68f0` received while bit 8 was up) pushed to the FRONT in order. `FUN_002b4c10` advances each of the two SMs by at most one job per poll. The level load is a CHAIN of jobs, each enqueued from the previous one's notify: the header read job (record `0x306f60`, `FUN_002b6050`) → `FUN_002b6b28` → `FUN_002b40f0` (count = header word0 & 0xffff; count ≠ 0 → the entry-table read job with record `0x306b28` whose `+0x14 = FUN_002b3f18`; count == 0 → the callback job `FUN_002ab740` at once) → `FUN_002b3f18` → `r19` sub-resource read jobs (record `0x2fbe00` from `FUN_001f5540`, `+0x14 = FUN_001f42b0` = the stage step; type 0 deserialises the head node) then the callback job (record `0x306b00`, `+0x14 = FUN_002b6018` → the notify `FUN_001f55c0`). **The invariant: with one job at a time and a FIFO, the callback job is dispatched only after every job ahead of it answered done — the stage steps precede the notify STRUCTURALLY; the only way to the thunk with `+0x144 = 0` is a header job that answered done with undelivered data (count 0 → `r19 = 0`).** *Dynamic (`w2/`, diag binary, `--watch-word 0xbb3560,0x3b3d00..0x3b3d5c,0x30a080,0x30a088`, `frames=309 park=fatal_no_jump`):* `b302` the host issue (fs `0x20300->0x30300`, slot 0 byte `0x3b3d00 0->2`), `b302 FUN_001e1f48+0x53b8 2->5`, `b303/304/305 FUN_001e1f48+0x50f4` countdown `0x3b3d30 3->2->1->0`, `b305 FUN_001e1f48+0x51a4` slot `5->0` (= done), `b306 FUN_002ad110+0x161c` fs `0x30300->0x20300` (the header job's completion — no `dlv`; the modelled end was tick 1497.8 = frame 309). **The NEW construction gap: the title's STREAM client (`FUN_001e1f48`, the reply processor run by every status call's end-callback) rewrites its slot table on every reply — `1 → 0` (countdown 3), `2 → 5`, `5 → countdown−−, 0 at zero`; a slot the reply LISTS is set to 1 (`3 → 4`); post-pass `4 → 3`, `3 → 0`. On the console the module lists a record in service in every reply, so its byte reads 1 until the module stops listing it. A host-served record is unknown to the recompiled module: no reply lists it and the client's own timeout retires the slot four replies after the issue — under poll visibility, where the modelled drive keeps a 1-sector long-seek read in flight for seven frames, the title's poll answers done over an undelivered window.** Not the queue, not the lift, not `CompleteBefore`, not the BUG-068 copy: the host's "issued" value was the wrong state for a record the module does not know. *Decision (MADE 09:21):* under `cdvd.delivery_visibility=poll` the async issue marks the slot `kSlotServed` (`0x10`, a value outside the client's state set {0..5}: `FUN_001e3308` and the other readers answer active = pending; every pass of `FUN_001e1f48` leaves it alone — the byte reads pending exactly until the delivery writes 0, the console's observable for a record the IOP is serving); boundary mode keeps `kSlotIssued` (2) — its lanes are frozen and a window there is delivered at the next boundary or the wait, before the countdown can fire (a latent class, rowed `INT-P82A-BOUNDARY-SLOT-AGING-LATENT`). Rejected: value 4 (the "re-listed" state; the post-pass turns it to 3 = done in the same reply); listing the record in the module's reply (a per-reply write into the module's buffer = a hook); re-asserting the byte after each reply (a hook on the end-callback). The toggle is the existing visibility key (one line: `served ? kSlotServed : kSlotIssued`). `0x10` is a stand-in value, not the console's byte (there the served slot reads 1 = listed in every reply); it is observationally equivalent for every reader verified (`FUN_001e3308`: 0/3 → done, 2/5 → queued, else active; `FUN_001e1f48`'s passes leave it; `FUN_001e6aa0/6af0` test `== 0`) and is NEW-only guest state under `poll` (review MINOR 4). Pins: `LoaderCompletionOrder` `aging_boundary_*` (the REAL `FUN_001e1f48` retires an issued slot in four unlisted replies while the read is in flight — the discriminator) and `aging_poll_*` (six replies leave the served byte and the countdown; pending until the poll that lands the read; done after); `RootCdvdPollVisibility` extended from 300 to 500 frames (past 309; TIMEOUT 900). *Measured:* the 309 park is GONE — the poll leg runs to frame 624 and then STOPS ADVANCING FRAMES (BUG-070, below). **Measured outcome: the DP-22 class (the pc=0 jump) is FIXED behind the toggle and pinned; three attempts spent, none OPEN. The score is the orchestrator's (the 12-leg battery); not KEPT by this stage.**

**DR-1 — Re-baselining under the OLD-defect rows (MADE 2026-10-02 23:40): NONE applied.** The rule allows a lane WINDOW to be re-baselined from NEW only
under a row naming the OLD defect and only when every residual word/key in the window is attributed. Measured against that rule: coldboot mem — boundaries
60 and 120 are fully attributed (DP-3, DP-4, DP-5, DP-8 (2)(b), DP-15, DP-19/DP-8 (5)) but boundaries 180…840 were never probed and boundaries ≥ 852 carry the
DP-17 class (NEW parks in boot case 6 while OLD reaches the title screen — a NEW shortfall, not an OLD defect): a two-line window changes no verdict and the
rest cannot be re-baselined honestly. Coldboot frames — the DP-4 (a) one-frame shift (`NEW[k] == OLD[k−1]`) holds for 63 of 101 keys in 69–169 and fails from
key 134 (the loading screen's progress differs: OLD's 22 reads/frame vs NEW's 1/frame = the DP-17 class again), 0 keys match after 170 (S0 rep 1 vs the
baseline): no honest window. Anchors — the probe residuals carry OPEN classes (DP-21 float operands, the pad `seq` format gap, the DP-20 restore phase both
sides guess), so the DP-19/DP-20 (a) rows cannot be applied as re-baselines. Consequence: the frozen OLD baselines stay the gate's reference unchanged;
Phase 8's regression guard is the 3-rep stability (12/12) plus the score tuple on every change.

## DP-23 — NEW owns the EE recompiler: OLD's `recompiler_tool_v2` is transplanted (byte-identical sources with provenance) into `tools/recompiler_v2/`, built in NEW's tree, and proved to regenerate `games/<title>/recomp/` byte-for-byte — the generator is now ours to fix (2026-10-03, P8.8-T)

**Evidence:** BUG-072 (P8.7-A, DP-18 status): the frozen pure-discovery generation splits the retail function `0x178470–0x178774` at its own branch delay slot (`FUN_00178470` + `FUN_0017847c`, whose `Label_0000` is the delay-slot `addiu sp,sp,-0x10` and whose switch has no `case 0x178480`), so every fall-through call leaks 0x10 of guest stack and the f57292/f59991 anchors stall in `FUN_0027edf0` once the walk's frame sits below the scratchpad. The defect is in the GENERATOR's output; `games/<title>/recomp/` is never hand-edited (CONSTRUCTION A.1 / rule 5.3) and until this stage its only producer was `OLD/build-v2-nog/recompiler_tool_v2` (md5 `6235a2f9e08121b4b694a870dcffc95c`), a binary of a frozen read-only tree that nobody may rebuild. The CONSTRUCTION_PLAN's "frozen OLD tool until Phase 9.1" rule (§A.1) therefore left no owner for the fix.

**Options:** (a) transplant the tool's sources into NEW (byte-identical copies with per-file provenance), build it in NEW, prove the unmodified copy reproduces the frozen shards byte-for-byte, then fix the discovery/emission rule in NEW and regenerate; (b) leave the generation frozen and add runtime glue for the split sites (a block label / entry shim for `0x178480` and every other delay-slot split); (c) feed the Ghidra function list to the frozen tool (`--ghidra-input`), reproducing OLD's single-function emission for `0x178470`.

**Choice: (a).** Rationale: the hardware rule is a MIPS one — an instruction in a branch's delay slot is executed with the branch and is never a function entry; the generator must encode it, not the runtime (b patches symptoms per site and keeps the defect class alive for every other split; c re-imports the Ghidra/overrides emission model the construction retired in Phase 2 and changes 9,327 vs 8,819 functions — not a fix, a different generation). (a) also closes the "frozen OLD tool" dependency: the tree now regenerates its own generated code (CONSTRUCTION A.1's Phase 9.1 goal, pulled forward; §A.1's wording is superseded by this entry — the regen protocol, inputs and manifests are unchanged).

**Toggle:** CMake option `PS2R_BUILD_RECOMPILER` (default ON; OFF builds the tree from the frozen output alone). `tools/regen_from_frozen.sh --tool <bin> --out <dir>` runs NEW's build; without `--tool` the script is unchanged (the frozen OLD binary, md5-gated).

**Measured outcome (P8.8-T, 2026-10-03; `int_work/p88t/`): KEPT — proof of identity.** 283 files (`tools/recompiler_v2/` 33 = `recompiler_tool/**`, `EEAnalyze/**`, OLD's `host_app/{cpu_state.h,memory.h}` + `include/json.hpp` (nlohmann, MIT, licence in-file) the tool compiles against, plus ONE NEW-authored file; `third_party/elfio/` 16 and `third_party/rabbitizer/` 234 (both MIT, LICENSE files copied)), every md5 in a `PROVENANCE.md` (OLD HEAD `5e1b17fe`; OLD's worktree clean for those paths — its only `git status` noise is untracked `.DS_Store` inside the ELFIO/rabbitizer submodules). **Deliberate deviation from "byte-identical" (review finding INT-P88T-PCSX2-GPL-HEADER):** OLD's `host_app/common/Pcsx2Types.h` is a verbatim PCSX2 file (`SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0+`), which STAGE_RULES rule 5 forbids in NEW; it is replaced by a NEW-authored header of the same name and type names (`s8..u64`, `uptr/sptr/uint`, `u128`/`s128` with `lo/hi` and the lane arrays, `==`/`!=`; md5 `2dc773d28beba4d52328d0fa69fdbd41`, row marked NEW-authored in PROVENANCE.md), so `cpu_state.h`/`memory.h`/`recompiler.cpp` stay byte-identical and the tool's OUTPUT is unaffected (it only compiles against those declarations — re-proved below with the rebuilt tool, md5 `9d94532c1f415faa5a818df41ee8a225`); built as `ps2r_recompiler_v2` (0 warnings, no SDL2 — OLD linked it, no source includes it). Two bounded runs (`verify/run_bounded.sh 1800`, ≈10 s each) with the frozen protocol (cwd pinned, the two stub inputs, `--shards 32 --direct-calls`, no `--ghidra-input`): `diff -r build/regen_new/a build/regen_new/b` differs ONLY in `recomp_gen.log` (the run's own absolute output paths `a/` vs `b/`), `diff -r -x cwd -x recomp_gen.log a b` EMPTY (exit 0); the literal `diff -r -x 'MANIFEST*' -x INVOCATION.md -x README.md build/regen_new/a games/<title>/recomp/` prints exactly four `Only in` lines and NO content difference — `Only in games/…/recomp: CMakeLists.txt` (NEW's build glue), `Only in build/regen_new/a: cwd` (the pinned scratch cwd), `Only in games/…/recomp: inputs` (the stub-input copies), `Only in build/regen_new/a: recomp_gen.log` (the bounded run's log) — none is generator output; with those four excluded the diff is EMPTY (exit 0); the 34 emitted-file SHA-256 lines of `MANIFEST.sha256` (36 lines incl. the two `inputs/`) verify (`shasum -a 256 -c` exit 0); the report lines (`discovered 8819 functions, 177 jump-table sites, 8848 unreached gap words`, `Auto-registered 36 tiny 'return N' stubs`) identical. Guards: ctest `RecompToolProvenanceIntact` (re-hashes the three trees against their PROVENANCE.md; fails on an edited copy or an unlisted file; TIMEOUT 60), `RecompManifestIntact` unchanged (shards untouched). Lints: `tools/recompiler_v2/` and `third_party/` exempt from LintOneClass (vendored/transplanted, not engine code); `CoreSingleGetenvSite` pins the 9 `getenv` TEXTS in the copies (7 generator string literals + 2 in OLD's `memory.h`) per file. **Next (P8.9-A):** the BUG-072 fix in `tools/recompiler_v2/recompiler_tool/discovery/` (a branch delay slot is never a leader/function entry) → regenerate with `--tool` → the golden diff names exactly the split sites → the two anchors to 600 frames.

**Status (P8.9-A, 2026-10-03): the first fix made in NEW's copy — BUG-072's delay-slot rule (DP-18 DECISION above). Three files diverge from OLD (`discovery/functions.cpp`, `discovery/discovery_types.h`, `main_v2.cpp`; `tools/recompiler_v2/PROVENANCE.md` rows carry the note and OLD's md5), `RecompToolProvenanceIntact` re-pinned; `tools/regen_from_frozen.sh` now defaults to NEW's tool (`--frozen-old` keeps OLD's binary for proofs); the title's `recomp/` is the P8.9-A generation (8807 functions, `MANIFEST.sha256` rewritten by the script).

## DP-24 — The pad slots' sequence counter after a savestate restore: the PADS decode derives it from the restored pad area (the console's PADMAN frame counter is IN the image); no savestate-format change; OLD's restart-at-1 is an OLD defect, rowed (2026-10-04, P8.11-A)

**Evidence (P8.11-A, `int_work/p811a/`):** INT-I86A-PAD-SEQ-NOT-SAVED named 4 words per anchor probe pair at `pad_area+0x58/+0xD8` (`0xbb05d8` / `0xbb0658`): OLD 5/6 vs NEW 4/5 at artdet b2, 63/64 vs 62/63 at b60. `seq_probe.py` on the images: the artdet ANCHOR holds slot 0 seq **1552** (slot 1 1553) — the console's counter at the save instant — and every anchor likewise (f36935 50336, f44111 58023, f57292 691561); OLD's frozen `old_b2` holds 5, `old_b60` 63. OLD `host_app/pad_input.cpp:829` `static uint32_t s_seq = 1; s_seq++;` — a process static, bumped once per pump (`:827-830`), which `PadInput_SerializeState` / `PadInput_RestoreState` (`:1015-1053`) neither save nor restore: OLD's 5 is its fresh process's counter (boot boundaries + the leg's), not a console value, and it over-counts NEW by one pump (OLD's `s_seq++` runs before the port loop, on boundaries with no port open too). NEW's `PadProtocol::seq_` had the same shape (1 at construction; `PadsSection::Restore` never set it). Libpad's reader picks the slot with the larger counter and the title's pad state machine needs the counter to ADVANCE (BUG-065's record), never its absolute value — so the title's behaviour is identical under every option; only the four words' values differ.

**Options:** (a) derive the counter at restore from the restored pad area (slot 0's +0x58 word of the first open port; the memory sections restore ahead of PADS) — no format change, every frozen anchor decodes the console's value; (b) add a PADS `seq` field (format v+1) with (a) as the decode of old-format images — a field that duplicates a word already in guest RAM; (c) copy OLD: restart at 1 and add OLD's extra boot pump to match 5/63 — reproduces a defect to buy 4 words.

**Choice: (a).** Rationale: the hardware rule — PADMAN's frame counter lives in the pad buffer it DMAs each vsync and is never restarted while the port is open; a restored console continues from the buffer's value. (b) is redundant state (the slot words ARE the counter; a field could disagree with them). (c) is forbidden by this policy (OLD is evidence, never the goal; a defect is rowed, not copied).

**Toggle:** `PadsSection::SeqRestore` (`FromImage` = the console, default; `Restart` = the original's restart-at-1), the one constructor line in `app/host/ee_bootstrap.cpp` (`PadsSection pads_section(pad_protocol, read32, PadsSection::SeqRestore::FromImage)`). Not a Config key: `engine/core/config/` was outside the stage's paths (INT-P811A-TOGGLE-NOT-CONFIG-KEY) — a key `pad.seq_restore` can wrap the same enum if the orchestrator wants a `--set` A/B. Census: `run.meta pad_seq=<live>,<derived>`.

**Measured outcome (P8.11-A, dev build `build/app/host/nativeee`, `score_probes.py` = `score.pair_words` with the gate mask): SCORE-NEUTRAL by construction, the 4 words become ATTRIBUTED.** artdet b2 `RAM0 64 SPAD 98` (S6: 64/98), b60 `400/97` (S6: 400/97); f36935 b2 `101/92`, b60 `136/92` (S6 same); f44111 b2 `1086/164`, b60 `2879/187` (S6 same) — the pad seq words now read `OLD=(5, 6) NEW=(1555, 1556)` at artdet b2 (anchor 1552 + 3 stamps), `(63, 64)` vs `(1613, 1614)` at b60, f36935 `(50339, 50340)` / `(50397, 50398)`, f44111 `(58026, 58027)` / `(58084, 58085)`. The four words per pair are an OLD-defect class (`P5-ANCHORS-OLD-PAD-SEQ-RESTART`, proposed for EQUIVALENCE_PLAN §7.1: OLD's never-saved process-static counter; evidence above) — never a mask (guest state). Pins: `PadProtocolRestore` (image N → N+1/N+2 on the next stamp; the `Restart` toggle publishes 2/3; no reader / no port / absent section arms), `RootAnchorPadReady` (`state_b0 seq slot0=1553 slot1=1554 (anchor 1552)`, `state_b2 … 1555`, `state_b3 … 1556`; RED on the S6 binary: `2/3`, `4`, `5`). Full suite 398/399 (the one failure is the sibling's untracked `verify/` file under `LintEnginePurity`, PASS on re-run); Release rebuilt, `RootReleaseLanesIdentical` Passed. **Status: landed (default = the console); KEEP/REVERT by the orchestrator's 12-leg score (expected neutral: same differing-word count, attributed).**

## DP-25 — The GS pipeline on its own consumer thread (plan row 6.3): the DrawIR ring + consumer thread land behind `gs.queue = inline | thread | pipelined`; `thread` runs in LOCKSTEP and is byte-identical by construction (pinned); the pipelined form is −19 % wall but NOT identical because the GS local-memory image is shared and the EE side settles / stamps it at every flush and transfer — the VRAM-image barrier sites are the remaining work, not the queue (2026-10-04, P8.12-A)

**Evidence (P8.12-A, `int_work/p812a/`):** I8.10-B measured the whole `DrawPipeline` path running synchronously inside the EE thread's GIF-kick MMIO write (`Dmac::Write32` 97 % of the EE thread, `DrawPipeline::Draw` 85 %). The plan's row 6.3 intended a RenderQueue the EE pushes into and a consumer/graphics thread that runs the pipeline, synchronised at slot 0 by the PresentRendezvous ticket. Reading the pipeline's inputs for a thread hand-off: (1) `DrawIr`/`JobRec` are values (copyable); (2) the upload payload is published by the transfer engine AFTER its `UploadVRAMTexture` push returns (`transfer_engine.cpp:139-141, 274-277`: `PushJob(...); last_upload_ = std::move(...)`), so the in-thread consumer applies it at the next event; (3) the CRTC snapshot is read from `GsPrivRegs` at the frame's `OnFrame`; (4) **the pipeline and the EE share ONE `VramImage`** (`video.Create(..., gs_state.Vram())`): the EE side writes it per pixel (`WriteStream`), stamps it (`NoteStreamWrite` at TRXDIR), **settles it before EVERY draw flush** (`gs_state.cpp:14-17` `SetBeforeFlushHook → vram_.SettleWrites()`, which also `CommitDeposits()` — the GPU side's staged writebacks are committed only at these EE observation boundaries, `vram_image.h:12-18`) and reads it raw for local→local copies (`LocalToLocal` → `vram_->Bytes()`), while the pipeline reads it (`ReadView` in the resolver / composer / readback) and stages deposits into it (`StageDeposit`). The deposit/commit rule is therefore interleaving-dependent: a consumer that lags the EE by even one record changes which deposits a settle commits and which bytes a decode reads.

**Measured (Debug `build/`, artdet `--frames 30`, `m30_*`):** inline `wall_ms=7623`; `thread` (lockstep) `8251` (+8.2 %: ≈ 13 µs per record round trip over 48768 records; `max_depth=1`) with frames/drawir/mem/gsops `SAME`; `pipelined` `6143` (−19.4 %; `max_depth=1482` ≈ a whole frame of run-ahead, `frame_waits=30`) with drawir/mem/gsops `SAME` but **frames.fhash 29/30 lines DIFFER** (`texres` census: `l1_hits=40521` vs `40611`, `decodes=319` vs `297` — the decode tiers saw different VRAM bytes), exactly the shared-image class above. Pin `RootGsQueueThreadIdentical` (coldboot 130 + artdet 60, inline vs thread): `pin=IDENTICAL` on all five lanes (coldboot `wall_ms` 4533 vs 4521; artdet 13734 vs 14894).

**Options:** (a) land the ring + consumer thread with the LOCKSTEP policy as `thread` (identical by construction; the GL context moves to the consumer thread; no throughput gain yet) and keep the pipelined policy as a measurement value; (b) pipelined by default with a `Sync()` barrier at every EE-side `VramImage` touch (the settle before each flush, TRXDIR, `WriteImage`, local→local, the `UploadEpoch()` stamp reads) — those sites are in `engine/gs/state` + `engine/gs/xfer`, outside this stage, and a barrier per flush is lockstep by another name: the real lever is page-granular (sync only when the EE touches pages the queued records read/deposit) or a snapshot/double-buffered image; (c) stay inline.

**Choice: (a).** Rationale: the hardware has one local memory and one GS; the in-thread interleaving IS the console's observation order (draws see the uploads that preceded them, the EE reads back what the GS wrote before the read). The thread form must reproduce that order to be a correct implementation of row 6.3; lockstep does, by construction, and lands the architecture (ring, consumer thread, frame ticket, GL affinity, main-thread-only window geometry deferred from the consumer — the first windowed `thread` run trapped in `SDL_SetWindowSize` on the consumer thread, `nativeee-2026-10-04-033918.ips`). The throughput lever is the VRAM image's dependency tracking (b), a GS-state-owned change with the pin already in place to adjudicate it.

**Toggle:** Config `gs.queue` = `inline` (default) | `thread` (lockstep) | `pipelined` (measurement only; identity not guaranteed — documented in `config.h`); CLI `--set gs.queue=thread`. Census `run.meta gs_queue=mode=…,policy=…,enqueued=,consumed=,frames=,syncs=,frame_waits=,max_depth=,payload_bytes=,deferred_resizes=`. Pins: `DrawIrThreadQueue{Lockstep,Pipelined,NoPayload}` (unit), `RootGsQueueThreadIdentical` (the five-lane byte compare in both modes), `RootPlayPolicyInert` unchanged (default inline).

**Measured outcome / KEEP rule:** the frames lane is identical under `thread`, so the 12-leg score is unchanged by construction; `thread` is KEPT as the default only when its play-mode throughput improves (today it does not: lockstep costs the hop). **Status: landed, default `inline`; the orchestrator flips `gs.queue=thread` on the throughput measurement once the VRAM-image barrier (option (b), page-granular) lands and `RootGsQueueThreadIdentical` stays green under the pipelined policy.** Play-mode window (`--frames 240`, windowed coldboot): see the P8.12-A record.

**DP-25 (b) — LANDED (2026-10-10, the s4 performance round, `port-graphics-r2` 9aed4588..e44a6030; record `int_work/m_series/graphics/PERF_S4_{1,2}.md`, in-tree PERFORMANCE.md §8).** The VRAM-image barrier was done as a SPLIT, not a per-site `Sync()`: the pipeline runs on its OWN `VramImage`; the EE keeps `GsState`'s; every EE-side call on the EE image (`WriteStream` runs coalesced, `NoteWriteNoBytes`, `NoteStreamWrite`, `SettleWrites`, `NoteCpuWrite`) is observed through `VramMirror` by a `VramOpLog` that `DrawIrThreadQueue` pushes as one record right before the next record (the point the in-thread consumer observed those writes) and replays on the pipeline image on the consumer thread — EE writes and GPU deposits interleave in stream order exactly as on the shared image. The EE's two reads of pipeline-side state drain the queue first (`TransferEngine::SetLocalToLocalSource`, `GsSections::SetVramSource`); owned producers' work runs on the consumer through `RunOrdered` (a barrier by construction); a consumer-thread fault is rethrown once on the EE thread (the park / triage path as inline). `inline` and `thread` keep one image. **Measured identity (the DP-25 pin extended to all-on, same binary, inline vs pipelined, five lanes):** `RootGsQueuePipelinedIdentical` (coldboot 200) in the suite; the s4 play-session replay `play_sessions/s4/session.padlog` 4000 and 6000 deterministic frames `pin=IDENTICAL` (frames 4000/6000 keys, mem, drawir.ohash, gsops.ohash, audio); the level route `tests/app/root_shadow_level_entry.padlog` 7600 frames `pin=IDENTICAL` (shadows + target reads + effects); cross-binary, `main` 9948796b inline vs HEAD pipelined `cmp`-identical on all five lanes. **Default: FLIPPED to `pipelined`** in the branch (config.h + settings table; the prepared patch `perf_s4/integration/perf_s4_gs_queue_default_pipelined.patch` applied), on the identity above (the user's criterion) and the idle A/B (`perf_s4/S4_IDLE_TABLE.md`, PERFORMANCE.md §8: before 96c8525c → after pipelined, s4 padlog 4000 frames, machine in Low Power Mode on battery: deterministic 28.6/28.2 → 25.2/24.9 ms/frame (−12 %); play pace_late 3829/3626/3630 → 2676/2723/2521, underruns 9451/2905/4264 → 1777/1967/1871, ms/frame 66.8/31.7/33.7 → 29.0/29.0/28.9 — the inline arms fell into a ≈ 65 ms/frame runaway in 2 of 6 play runs, the pipelined arm never; pace_late → 0 unreachable under the throttle for any arm); `--set gs.queue=inline` is the one-line override. `RootGsQueueThreadIdentical` (inline vs `thread`) refuses its artdet leg under all-on exactly as the frozen 96c8525c binary does (`--load-state artdet_f37152.ps2st: Restore needs owned logical clock state.` — the frozen-anchor class, recorded, not weakened); its coldboot half is identical.

## DP-26 — The anchors' first frame after a restore (frames key 1 divergent on all 11): NEW restored NO GS section (VRAM / GSPR / GSST were "the GS owner's, skipped until it lands"), so the restore began with empty local memory and reset display registers; the GS sections are now restored (GsSections; the console's state), OLD's key 1 is its boot-era pre-load present (value 0 by construction) and needs a pairing rule, and the NEW grab at the restore point reads the title's render target, not the DISPFB buffer (2026-10-04, P8.13-A)

**Evidence** (`int_work/p813a/`): every frozen anchor baseline opens `1,0x0000000000000000` (`verify/baseline/<anchor>/frames.fhash` line 1, all 11) — OLD's graphics thread presents once before the load with no target (`verify_present.cpp:60-62` "hash==0 marks a present with no readable scanout") and reseeds its counter at the load (`graphics_thread.cpp:2276-2281`: `s_frame_count = reseed; s_frame_count++`), so OLD's first POST-load present is keyed `restored_vsync_frame + 1` (artdet `37157`) and line 1 is a pre-load event. S6's NEW key 1 on all 11 anchors was `0xa741b33a5c037124` = a **512x448 BLACK frame** (`fhash_py.py`: `512x448 black: 0xa741b33a5c037124`; the hasher was reproduced in Python and checked against OLD: the OLD `PS2_SNAP` display dump `disp_F37157.bmp` hashes to `0xe8bf7cac1b6ae3a7` = baseline key 37157 exactly, and NEW's own grab dumps hash to NEW's lane values). Cause: `app/host/ee_bootstrap.cpp` registered no VRAM / GSPR / GSST provider ("the registry skips them as unknown until it lands"), so a restored leg had zero local memory, DISPFB/DISPLAY/PMODE at reset and the drawing environment at power-on until the title rewrote them. The anchors' GSPR (`gspr_dump.py`): PMODE `0x8023` (EN1|EN2, ALP 128), both DISPFB `0x11000` = FBP 0 / FBW 8 (512 px) / PSMCT16, DISPLAY 2560/5 x 448, SMODE2 INT=1; GSST v3 (`gsst_dump.py`): TEXA ta0 128 / ta1 0, PRMODECONT 0 with PRMODE 0x150, FBA ctx0 = 1, FRAME 0x70000 CT32 512 px, ZBUF 0x150000 — init-once registers the power-on values do not match.

**What the hardware does:** a restore is instantaneous state; the first vblank after it scans out the buffer DISPFB names out of local memory — the frame the title last finished — while the title renders its next frame; the first newly rendered frame is scanned out one vblank later. OLD never presents that restore-point frame (its line 1 predates the load); NEW's boundary 0 IS the restore point (`state_b0` = boundary 0, I8.5-G), so NEW's key 1 is a real event OLD has no line for.

**Options:** (a) restore the GS sections and present the restored scanout at key 1 (the console); pair key 1 by a rule, not by value (OLD's 0 is a pre-load artefact: DP-9 (c) class); (b) emit 0 at NEW's key 1 to mimic OLD (models OLD's artefact; rejected); (c) skip the restore-point present (NEW's boundary 0 would present nothing — but the console does scan out at every vblank; rejected).

**Choice (a), landed in two parts.** (1) `engine/gs/savestate/gs_sections.{h,cpp}` (`GsSections` over `GsState`, built into `ps2r_gs_state`): VRAM (4 MiB → `GsState::RestoreLocalMemory`: `WriteStream` + `SettleWrites` + ONE `InvalidateTextures` job over [0, 4 MiB)), GSPR (19 × u64 written through `GsPrivRegs::Write` — DISPFB/DISPLAY/BGCOLOR emit their presenter jobs — CSR via the new `RestoreCsr`; the video-mode triple kept), GSST (the decoded register file: PRIM + attributes, both contexts, the environment incl. TEXA/PRMODECONT/PRMODE/FBA/PABE/SCANMSK/FOGCOL, the RGBAQ/ST/UV/FOG latches; v1/v2/v3 payloads, the latched vertex records skipped, the v3 K/MIPTBP tail read); all three REQUIRED; registered in the root after VIFS; census `gs_sections_restored=<vram>/<gspr>/<gsst>`, `gs_restored_dispfb1=`. Pins `GsSections{Roundtrip,Jobs,Refuse,Registry,Gsst}` (85 checks). (2) The frames-lane pairing rule for the orchestrator (`verify/` is P8.13-B's): **for a `tree=legacy` A vs NEW B restored-anchor pair, key 1 is NOT a value pair — A's line 1 is the pre-load present (0 by construction), B's line 1 is the restore-point scanout; `diff_legs` reports it as `restore-point present (OLD has none)` and counts it on the verdict line (never silently), the value comparison starts at key `restored_vsync_frame + 1`.** Toggle: the three `GsSections` registrations in `ee_bootstrap.cpp` (one line each).

**Measured outcome.** All 11 anchors now carry a non-black key 1 (`anchors3/summary.txt`: `gs_sections_restored=1/1/1` on all; artdet `1,0x9aada0a287ced70c`), the dev + Release builds are 0 warnings, the suite is `100% tests passed, 0 tests failed out of 413` (loop 1; 412 before the root pin), `RootReleaseLanesIdentical` Passed. **Root pin (loop 1, the review's MAJOR #1):** `RootAnchorGsRestored` (`tests/app/root_frames_pairing.py --mode gs_restored`, TIMEOUT 150, SKIP 77) runs the 3-frame restored artdet leg and asserts `gs_sections_restored=1/1/1`, `gs_restored_dispfb1=0x11000` and key-1 hash ≠ `0xa741b33a5c037124` (512x448 black); RED with the registration line removed — measured: `gs_sections_restored=0/0/0`, `gs_restored_dispfb1=0x0`, key 1 `0xa741b33a5c037124`, `RESULT: FAIL (3)` — GREEN with it (`Passed 4.22 sec`). The exact pin (key 1 == the anchor's DISPFB decode) stays BUG-076's OPEN item. The rendered frames from key `restored_vsync_frame + 1` are byte-identical before and after the GSST restore (artdet 37157 `0x464ce75c4932bfe4` both) — the title rewrites its drawing environment at frame start, so GSST changes no pixel today (kept: it is the console's state). **Status: landed, score to be measured by the orchestrator (frames key 1 moves only once the pairing rule lands in `diff_legs`; mem/audio untouched by construction — GS local memory is not in the mem lane).** **P8.14-A (2026-10-04): the pairing rule LANDED in `verify/diff_legs.py` (`restore_point_rule`): when exactly one leg is `tree=legacy` with line 1 `1,0x0` and the other's `run.meta` has `restored_vsync_frame=<vf>` ≠ 0, key 1 is listed (`frames.fhash: restore-point present (OLD has none): key 1 -- A: 1,0x0… (the original's pre-load present …), B: 1,0x… (the restored scanout); values compared from key vf+1 …`) and carried on the verdict line (`…, coalesced 0, restore-point present (OLD has none): key 1)`); cold boots, a non-zero legacy line 1 and same-tree pairs are untouched; a restored leg carrying a key between 1 and vf is a named lane error. 11 selftest checks; measured on the 11 anchors: every verdict moved from `FIRST DIVERGENT frame at key 1 … (0 keys paired before it)` to `FIRST DIVERGENT frame at key vf+1 … restore-point present (OLD has none): key 1` (`int_work/p814a/anchors3/summary.txt`). Finding (i) above is RE-ATTRIBUTED — see DP-27: the grab reads the title's own display-buffer target at the scanout base (`gs_key1_grab=target@0x0`, 1,948 draws before boundary 0), whose content is the title's copy of the anchors' stale `0x70000` pages (finding (ii)); the pin "key 1 == the anchor's DISPFB decode" is withdrawn (the saved DISPFB bytes are overwritten before the first vblank).**

**Two further findings the measurement produced (both OPEN, named):** (i) **NEW's restore-point grab is NOT the DISPFB buffer.** The key-1 hash is one of TWO values across the 11 anchors (`0x9aada0a287ced70c` on 7, `0xd2f8701bca985574` on 4) although every anchor's VRAM at DISPFB (FBP 0, CT16) decodes to a different picture (`vram_scanout.py`: artdet `0xe228dcdf0969ad2d`, f46949 `0x5a37b4a2e4d4c725`, f40391 `0xbc1f1b7393b82fc7`, …); the dumped f40391 key-1 grab IS the artdet scene. The grab reads the GPU target the composer falls back to — the title's CT32 render target at FRAME `0x70000` refreshed from the restored VRAM — not the CT16 display buffer at DISPFB 0 (no GPU target exists there before the first draw; `ScanoutComposer::GrabDisplay` has no VRAM-decode arm, `DrawScanout` has). The console rule for the grab: when no target exists at the scanout base, decode the DISPFB tuple from local memory (the mirror arm `DrawScanout` already owns) — the pin "key 1 == the anchor's DISPFB decode" (artdet: `0xc243d706bc18cc7c` with the `c << 3` expansion the refresh path uses, flipped rows; 228,441 / 229,376 pixels of the artdet grab equal the FBP-0 decode because its two buffers held the same frame) is RED today → BUG-076 (OPEN, the next construction step in `engine/gs/hle/present/`). (ii) **OLD's anchors carry a STALE render-target region in their VRAM section (OLD defect, row candidate `P5-ANCHORS-STALE-RT-VRAM`).** artdet (vf 37156) and f40391 (vf 40391, the same OLD session, 3,200 frames later) hold BYTE-IDENTICAL local memory over `0x70000-0x280000` (pages 56–319: the CT32 render target at FRAME 0x70000 and the ZBUF at 0x150000) while their DISPFB CT16 buffers differ (`vram_region_cmp.py`: `FRAME 0x70000 CT32 512x448: identical=True`, `ZBUF 0x150000: identical=True`, `DISPFB0 CT16: identical=False`): OLD's `SyncAllRTsToVRAM` at the save did not write the live render target back, so the anchors' render-target pages are an older frame's. On the console those pages hold the frame just rendered. Any NEW (or OLD) consumer of the restored render target — the first frame's depth/colour feedback, a previous-frame read — sees artdet-era bytes; a row (OLD defect: the VRAM section's GPU-authored pages were not synced at the save) is proposed, not written (`docs/design/` not owned).

## DP-27 — The display grab when no target sits at the scanout base: local-memory decode (the CRTC's read) vs the original's most-recently-drawn fallback; and the restore-point grab re-attributed (2026-10-04, P8.14-A)

**Evidence** (`int_work/p814a/`): (1) on every anchor the key-1 grab reads a colour target AT the scanout base — census `gs_key1_grab=target@0x0`, `gs_grab_sources=local_memory=0,fallback=0` on all 11 (`anchors3/summary.txt`); a `--frames 1` artdet leg counts `drawir=draws=1948` BEFORE boundary 0 (`art1/run.meta`; frame 2 adds 1,649): the restored title resumes mid-frame, draws its display copy from FRAME `0x70000` into the DISPFB buffer (FBP 0, CT16) and renders, all before its first vblank. The key-1 hash groups (`0x9aada0a287ced70c` on artdet/f39807/f40391/f44111/f46203/f46949/slowspot_f40298, `0xd2f8701bca985574` on f36935/f57292/f59991/slowspot_f37092) coincide with the anchors whose `0x70000-0x280000` pages are byte-identical (`p813a/vram_region_cmp.py`: artdet = f40391 `FRAME 0x70000 CT32: identical=True`; f57292 = f59991 `identical=True`; f57292 vs artdet `identical=False`, ZBUF identical even across sessions) — the copy's source is the anchors' STALE render target (DP-26 (ii), OLD saver defect). (2) The title's frame-start environment packet (OLD `logs/runtime_log.txt:2927-2975`, the 46-register A+D packet at `0x3ff1c0` at F:37156: TEXFLUSH, **DTHE = 0**, PRMODECONT 1, XYOFFSET_2, BITBLTBUF, TRXREG, COLCLAMP 1, FRAME_1 = 0, PRMODE, SCISSOR_2, PABE, then PRIM 0x306 sprites with RGBAQ 0 over FBP 0) rewrites the environment every frame — DTHE is OFF on both trees at every rendered frame, so the "dither" half of INT-P813A-FRAME-TONE-CLASS is not the GS dither (DTHE/DIMX are not in GSST and need not be: the title programs them per frame). (3) The local-memory arm on a cold boot (`cold240/run.meta`, `--frames 240`): `gs_key1_grab=local_memory@0x70000`, `gs_grab_sources=local_memory=61,fallback=0` — the first 61 presents decode the empty local memory at the boot DISPFB tuple to the 512x448 black frame (`0xa741b33a5c037124`) where the original's lines (and NEW's before) read `0x0000000000000000` ("no readable scanout", OLD `verify_present.cpp:60-62`); keys 62–240 byte-identical to gate S7's `coldboot.rep1` (`diff` shows only keys 1–61). S7's coldboot frames lane pairs 66 keys before its first divergence at key 69; with the arm it would pair none before key 1.

**What the hardware does:** the CRTC scans out the buffer DISPFB names out of local memory at every vblank (GS User's Manual 6 / 8.1) — an empty local memory shows black, and a buffer the title just drew shows the title's draw. The grab's target-at-the-scanout-base rule IS that read whenever a GPU target holds the display buffer's authoritative bytes; when none does, the console still shows local memory (black at boot; the saved display buffer only if the title drew nothing — it always draws its copy first, so on the anchors that case never arises).

**Options:** (a) `GrabPolicy::LocalMemory` as the live root's policy (the console; a cold boot's first 61 presents become the black frame → a new frames-lane class vs the original's `0x0`, 61 keys, needs its own pairing rule of the DP-9 (c) kind); (b) `GrabPolicy::TargetFallback` (the original's pick: the most recently drawn target stands in; `0x0` when none — matches the original's artefact by construction); (c) prefer local memory over an existing target at the base (rejected: the GPU target holds bytes the mirror does not yet, and the title's copy is real).

**Choice: the arm landed (`ScanoutComposer::GrabPolicy`, `GrabLocalMemory` = the display quad's mirror decode `TextureDecoder::DecodeScanoutBuffer` over the DISPFB tuple and the content dims, after the display footprint's deferred deposits landed, rows flipped into the attachment's bottom-up order so both sources hash alike; `Presenter::Grab(…, vram)`, `DrawPipeline::Frame::{source, source_fbp, source_fallback}`), default (b) in the live root — the ONE-LINE TOGGLE is `video.Pipe()->Scanout().SetGrabPolicy(ps2r::gs::ScanoutComposer::GrabPolicy::TargetFallback)` in `app/host/ee_bootstrap.cpp` (one word to `LocalMemory`); the composer's own default stays `TargetFallback` (the corpus replay's grab is untouched).** Pins: `Present_GrabLocalMemory` (16 checks: bottom-up rows, replicate widening, tuple/dims gates, the colour target at the base wins, undisplayable PSM and the default policy fall back); `RootAnchorGsRestored` now also asserts `gs_key1_grab=target@0x0` and prints the anchor's DISPFB decode (`tests/app/anchor_dispfb_decode.py`) for the record. Census: `gs_key1_grab=<target|local_memory|none>@<fbp>[,fallback]`, `gs_grab_sources=local_memory=N,fallback=N`.

**Measured outcome.** Anchors: no change by construction (neither arm engages — `local_memory=0,fallback=0` on all 11; key 1 and vf+1… byte-identical to P8.13-A's `anchors3`). Cold boot with (a): 61 keys change from `0x0` to the black frame; the S7 coldboot frames lane would pair 0 keys before key 1 instead of 66 (score tuple unchanged: no leg is identical either way; the per-leg first-divergent key regresses) → (a) is NOT the default; the orchestrator may flip it together with a `diff_legs` rule for the original's `0x0` = "no readable scanout" presents (the DP-9 (c) class: the console shows black, the original hashed nothing). **Status: landed, default = the original's pick (TargetFallback); DP-26 finding (i) re-attributed: NOT a NEW gap. The anchors' key-1 content is the OLD saver's (row candidate `P5-ANCHORS-STALE-RT-VRAM`, DP-26 (ii)); the console would show the title's copy of the frame it finished before the save.**

**The GENUINE record class (P8.13-A Part 2, carried; structural facts only — the field/writer attribution is not done):** `int_work/p813b/ledger/ps2state_slot0_f46949_yellow_crystal_b60.json`: the heap and pool layouts are IDENTICAL between OLD and NEW at b60 (heap `identical_layout_blocks=541/541`, `alloc_calls=1941` both; pool `idx_pairs_equal=1184/1184`, `moved=0`, `old_only=[]`, `new_only=[]`; records `matched=1717`, `equal_under_relocation=1711`, `old_only=[]`, `new_only=[]`), so the 5,906 GENUINE words (first `0x3fd774`, inside the heap arena `0x3dae90-0xb84be0`) are CONTENT differences inside **6 shared records**, not NEW-only objects from completed loads — the ZERO-POLLS consequence class is ruled out for this pair by the layout identity. Next: name the 6 records (vtable / size from the slab walk), their differing fields and the writers (`word_watch` on the first differing word of each record over the 60 frames), then class per record (OLD defect → row text, or NEW gap).

## DP-28 — The anchors' first rendered frame (key vf+1, the first divergent key on all 11 after DP-26): the difference is the title's CT32→CT16 display copy stored at 8 bits by OLD (the GS stores 5) — an OLD defect, row proposed; the residual is sub-step render noise plus a few object regions, OPEN (2026-10-04, P8.15-A)

**Evidence** (`int_work/p815a/`; OLD read-only, run bounded with its own recorders: `PS2_SNAP` display dump + `PS2_DRAWLIST_RECORD` with `_PPM=1 _VERTS=1`, every path pinned under the work dir; the leg's `frames.fhash` = the frozen baseline lines 1–4 exactly, so the recorders changed no lane value; NEW through the new `--dump-frames <dir>` knob, every dump proved by hash). (1) **The dumps ARE the presented images:** `tools/frame_diff.py old_snap/disp_F37157.bmp new_art3/frames/f37157.ppm` → `A … fhash=0xe8bf7cac1b6ae3a7` (= baseline key 37157), `B … fhash=0x464ce75c4932bfe4` (= NEW's lane key 37157); OLD's `PS2_DRAWLIST_RECORD_PPM` frame `f037157.ppm` has the identical value lattice to its `PS2_SNAP` BMP (same 250/256/250 distinct values per channel). (2) **The value lattice** (`value_lattice.py`): NEW's frame has exactly **32 distinct values per channel, all `≡ 0 mod 8`** (`q5 << 3`: a PSMCT16 frame's 5-bit channels, zero-fill widened; max 248); OLD's frame has **250 / 256 / 250 distinct values** — an 8-bit image. The display buffer is PSMCT16 (`DISPFB1 = 0x11000`: FBP 0, PSM 2; the title's display copy is frame draw[0] `sprite+tex n=96 fbp=0x000000/CT16 fbw=512 ← TEX0 tbp=0x070000 CT24 1024x1024 tfx=0 fst=1 mmag=0 mmin=0`, `drawlist_tool dump --frame 1`). A PSMCT16 frame store keeps 5 bits per channel (GS User's Manual, PSMCT16 = RGBA 5551; with DTHE = 0 — the title writes DTHE 0 every frame, DP-27 (2) — the low bits are dropped); OLD's displayed values cannot exist in that buffer. (3) **The class explains the whole-frame tone shift:** raw diff 216,583 / 229,376 px (94.4 %), per-channel mean B−A = −3.48 / −3.64 / −3.43 (= the expected −3.5 of a 3-bit truncation); after truncating OLD's frame to the manual's rule (`quantize_ppm.py … zero`) the means are **−0.05 / −0.15 / −0.02** and the residual is 163,642 px (71.3 %), **100 % multiples of 8** — 102,449 px differ by exactly one 5-bit step, 32,785 by two; the pre-quantisation images differ by a few LSBs almost everywhere (two shader pipelines' sub-step rounding; the GS corpus proves NEW's renderer hash-identical to OLD's on OLD's batched streams — 18/19 captures — so the sub-step residue is live-stream granularity / deposit timing, not the renderer) plus a handful of regions with max |Δ| 104–152 (64-px grid cells at (128..256, 64..128) = Crash's skinned mesh, (320..384, 320..384) = the plant) — those are object-level (skinned-class) differences, OPEN. (4) **Shared across scenes:** f46949 (vf 46949, key 46950): OLD `0xee41af489c3949d7` (= baseline) 248/256/246 distinct values vs NEW `0xe15cd604aeac002c` 32/32/32 `≡ 0 mod 8`; raw 214,650 px, means −3.41 / −3.75 / −2.56; after truncating OLD: +0.07 / −0.32 / +1.15, residual 162,859 px all multiples of 8 (96,552 one step). Same class, same signature. (5) **The streams:** `gs_replay classdiff old.gsdl drawlist.gsdl` → every class's vertex count IDENTICAL (frame 1: 92,634 verts both; `d_verts=0` on all six classes); OLD's native class renderers coalesce (skinned 15 vs 804 draws, world-tristrip 451 vs 3,945 over 3 frames, `d_draws` only); `FGE = 0` on every draw of both trees (fog ruled out, `gsdl_census.py`); the vertex RGBA census is identical.

**What the hardware does:** the GS writes a PSMCT16 frame as 5-5-5-1; the CRTC scans those 5-bit channels out. Every pixel of the console's display is one of 32 levels per channel. OLD's 8-bit display is OLD's host-side approximation (its RT-sourced full-screen copy bypasses its own `[WN4-CT16Q]` write rule: `s9_shadow.h:90-104` "Default ON", `opengl_backend.cpp:2154-2185` — the quantiser is excluded when GL fixed-function blending consumes the output and is not on its copy paths; the measurement proves the displayed result). NEW's `gl_shader_sources.cpp:926-960` stores `q5 << 3` for every CT16-class frame write (the zero-fill form the title's shadow recipe depends on; §7.1 P2-EXP16-REPL note).

**Options:** (a) keep NEW's 5-bit store (the hardware) and row the OLD defect for the frames lane: the anchors' key vf+1… can never pair by value against OLD's 8-bit frames — the lane compares against OLD's own quantised frame only through a §7.1 row; (b) mimic OLD (an 8-bit display buffer) — rejected: models OLD's artefact, breaks the shadow recipe's byte arithmetic. **Choice (a); nothing to toggle (no NEW change: no GS gap found).** Row proposed for `docs/design/EQUIVALENCE_PLAN.md` §7.1 (not owned; text): **`P8-CT16-DISPLAY-8BIT` | frames | OLD displays a PSMCT16 buffer at 8 bits per channel (250–256 distinct values; artdet 37157, f46949 46950 measured) where the GS stores 5 (32 values, `q<<3`); the title's display copy (`sprite+tex` CT24-view of FRAME 0x70000 into FBP 0 CT16) is the draw; OLD's `[WN4-CT16Q]` rule is bypassed on that path. | Accepted difference (OLD defect): the frames lane compares a restored anchor's rendered frames against OLD only after truncating OLD's frame to 5 bits per channel (`v & 0xF8`, the manual's DTHE = 0 store); the residual (all multiples of 8; 71 % of pixels one or two steps) is the sub-step class below.** The secondary widening question (zero-fill `q<<3` vs replicate) is P2-EXP16-REPL's and stays there.

**Measured outcome.** No NEW change → the score is unchanged by construction. The frames lane's first divergent key stays vf+1 on all 11 anchors until the row lands (a row-aware compare is `verify/`'s, not this stage's). **Status: attributed — Class A (OLD defect, row proposed), Class B OPEN (sub-step render residue after the row: ±1–2 steps on 71 % of pixels + object-level regions; next: dump NEW's CT32 target at 0x70000 before the copy and OLD's `PS2_SNAP_RT=70000` dump of the same frame, diff at 8 bits; then the skinned class's draws over the (128..256, 64..128) cell).**

Writers (`--watch-word`, diag, `--frames 61 --watch-record-from 55`, `watch_f469/watch.log`): `0x3fd770/0x3fd7f0` ← **`FUN_00294b18+0xe70` then `FUN_00296228+0x28e0`** (8-byte stores, every other frame; `0x00294b18` is in `games/…/verify/manual_body_ledger.tsv` as OLD's **`anim_pose_hle.cpp` "anim-pose native mirror"** — OLD ran a hand-written host-float body there, NEW runs the v2 recompiled MIPS FPU/VU0 sequence (`FUN_00296228` carries `qmtc2`/`sqc2`/VU0 macro ops)); `0xad6ce0/0xad4750` ← **`FUN_001ca9c8+0x1fe0`** (8-byte stores, alternate frames; FPU-only recompiled body, 69 FPU ops); `0x98c250` ← **`FUN_001833d0+0x3800`** (4-byte, every frame, the value alternating 127.37 / 127.387; FPU-only). **Class: FLOAT-ROUNDING** — the same per-frame animation/skinning arithmetic rounded differently on the two trees: (i) the arena record's writer was OLD's native mirror (host float order/contraction) vs NEW's MIPS-exact recompiled sequence — the ledger's retirement gate names exactly this case ("a divergence attributed to the address → a P4FIX record, never a transplanted body"); (ii) the FPU-only writers differ in the two recompiler runtimes' FPU rounding (EE COP1 non-IEEE: no denormals, non-fused MADD, `ps2_float_clamp`) — which side is the hardware needs a MIPS-exact oracle (the PCSX2 interpreter's values at the same frame). Nothing decided: OPEN, proposed as the ledger class `FLOAT-ROUNDING` with the writers above; the ZERO-POLLS consequence class stays ruled out (layout identical). slowspot_f37092 b2 (4,577) / f40391 / f44111 / f57292 b60 not opened (time).

## DP-29 — FMV sound: the console programs the SPU2 core-0 external-input volume BVOLL/BVOLR = 0x7FFF at movie start (the title's own EE code, through sceSdRemote rSdSetParam 0xF80/0x1080) and back to 0 at movie end; NEW's model receives both, but the mixer was pumped only at EE frame boundaries, none of which pass during a movie, so the queued PCM was mixed only after BVOL returned to 0 — silence; the mixer is now pumped once per display field in play mode (device only; the hash lane keeps 800 frames per boundary); the baseline's silent FMV window is an OLD defect, row proposed (2026-10-04, P8.16-A)

**Evidence** (`int_work/p816a/`). (1) **The console (PCSX2 fork with its DebugServer, `start_button_before_fmv.p2s`):** the EE flag `0x30a934` reads `01 00 00 00` (the title's "sound driver ready" gate); an IOP write watchpoint on the SPU2 registers `0x1f90076c..0x1f900770` (core-0 BVOLL/BVOLR, PCSX2 `SPU2/regs.h` `REG_P_BVOLL 0x76C` / `REG_P_BVOLR 0x76E`) did not trip and debug reads of the register block return 0 (hardware-register writes bypass the memcheck; the debug read does not reach the SPU2 model), so the path was measured on the EE side: breakpoint `0x2af458` (`FUN_002af458`, the title's movie-volume setter) hit with `ra=0x002AE628` (its caller `FUN_002ae318` = the title's movie-audio start, right after its `rSdBlockTrans 0x80E0` call), `f12=1.000000`, `s4=0x310000`; breakpoint `0x2c4cf0` (`sceSdRemote`) then hit twice: `a0=1 a1=0x8010 a2=0x0F80 a3=0x7FFF ra=0x2af4a0` and `a1/a2/a3 = 0x8010 / 0x1080 / 0x7FFF`. The title's volume table at `0x3d6ba0` reads `00 10 00 10 …` (four `{0x1000, 0x1000}` entries = gain 1.0 per category, written by the sound manager's constructor `FUN_001df658`); `FUN_002af458(vol)` = `if (*0x30a934 == 1) { v = (int)(vol × 32767.0f); sceSdRemote(1, 0x8010, 0xF80, v); sceSdRemote(1, 0x8010, 0x1080, v); }` (`recompiled_shard_029.cpp` `FUN_002af458`, `FUN_002ae318` `0x2ae5cc..0x2ae628`). The SDK entry encoding matches NEW's decode: entry = `core | (param_index << 8)` with bit 7 marking a core attribute; `SD_P_BVOLL = 0x0F << 8`, `SD_P_BVOLR = 0x10 << 8` → `SpuCore::kBvoll = 0xF`, `kBvolr = 0x10` are the right shadow indices. All breakpoints/watchpoints cleared (`list_breakpoints` ee + iop empty). (2) **NEW, deterministic coldboot `--frames 200` (`det200/run.meta`, the census added by this stage):** `fmv_pcm=…bvol=0x0/0x0,bvol_sets=8,bvol_nonzero_sets=4,bvol_peak=0x7fff,mixed_audible=0` with `snd_pumps=200` — the EE issues the console's eight sets (two movies × BVOLL/BVOLR × {0x7FFF at start, 0 at end}), the libsdr row forwards them (`SdrRemote::Dispatch` → `SpuCore::SetParam` → `core_param_shadow_[0][0xF/0x10]`), the peak is the console's value — and NOT ONE FMV frame was mixed while the gain was non-zero: the movie spans ≈1100 fields without an EE frame boundary (the pace park advances the domain tick, the Verify slot never runs), so the boundary pumps that consume the queued PCM run after the title's end-of-movie `BVOL = 0`. The earlier reading "the title never programs BVOL" (I7.2-A INT-I72A-FMV-AUDIO-SILENT-BVOL0, i84b INT-I84B-FMV-PCM-GAIN0-BOTH) sampled the shadow at frame 400, after the end-of-movie zero. (3) **NEW play mode `--frames 200`, before the change (`play200/run.meta`, census only):** `snd_pumps=200`-class starvation: `audio_ring=…pulled=2419200,underruns=4034`, `mixed_audible=0`.

**What the hardware does:** the SPU2 mixes 48 000 stereo frames a second on its own clock; the core's external input (the IOP's PCM block transfer, `rSdBlockTrans`) enters the output at BVOLL/BVOLR, whether or not the EE passes a frame boundary. Movie audio on the console is heard because BVOL is 0x7FFF for the whole movie while the SPU2 keeps mixing.

**Options:** (a) pump the mixer once per DOMAIN TICK (one display field) in play mode — the boundary's own hashed pump covers the boundary's tick; every display tick (the FMV pace park's parked fields, a late serve, the kernel timer wait, the disc wait) catches the mixer up to the fields the domain advanced, each quantum pushed to the device ring only; the hash lane unchanged (800 frames per EE boundary, the baseline's shape). (b) pump per field in every mode and hash per field — rejected: changes the audio lane's window keys (the frozen baseline is per boundary) for no equivalence gain. (c) pump on the device callback (wall clock) — rejected: puts a wall clock into the mixer domain (the settled clock model). **Choice (a).** Toggle: `audio_field_pump_on` in `app/host/ee_bootstrap.cpp` (one line; `false` = the original's boundary-only pump). Not a Config key: `tools/gen_settings_table.py` and the settings table are outside the stage's paths (same disposition as DP-24's toggle); a key `audio.field_pump` can wrap the bool. Census: `run.meta snd_pumps_fields=<n>` (field pumps), `fmv_pcm=…bvol_sets=,bvol_nonzero_sets=,bvol_peak=,mixed_audible=`, `audio_device=quanta=,nonsilent=`.

**Measured outcome (play mode `--frames 200`, `play200b/run.meta`, 53 s wall):** `snd_pumps=2650 snd_pumps_fields=2450`, `fmv_pcm=pushed=897024,…,mixed=897024,…,queued=0,…,bvol_peak=0x7fff,mixed_audible=897024` — every movie PCM frame mixed while BVOL was 0x7FFF (before: `mixed_audible=0`); `audio_ring=pushed=2120000,dropped=644224,pulled=2522112,underruns=2167` (underruns 4034 → 2167; the drops are catch-up bursts after host stalls longer than the ring's 8 quanta — the ring's drop-oldest contract, recorded as a finding, not changed). Deterministic lanes byte-identical by construction (`det200` vs `det200b`: `frames.fhash IDENTICAL (200)`, `audio.ahash IDENTICAL (34)`, `mem.mhash IDENTICAL (4)`, `gsops.ohash`/`drawir.ohash` IDENTICAL; `snd_pumps_fields=0`; `RootPlayPolicyInert` Passed). Pins: `FmvPcmInput` (+14 checks: the BVOL census and the audible-frame count), `RootMixesFmvPcm` (+3: `bvol_peak=0x7fff`, `bvol_nonzero_sets >= 2`, `snd_pumps_fields=0` on the deterministic leg), `RootPlayFmvPresents` (+5: `bvol_peak=0x7fff`, `bvol_nonzero_sets >= 2`, `mixed_audible > 0`, `snd_pumps_fields > 1000`, `audio_device nonsilent > 0`). **Status: landed (default on in play mode; deterministic legs untouched). KEEP/REVERT by the orchestrator's 12-leg score — expected neutral by construction (no deterministic lane reads the field pump). The human audible check is the user's.**

**OLD-defect row proposed for `docs/design/EQUIVALENCE_PLAN.md` §7.1 (not owned; text):** **`P7-FMV-BVOL-OLD-UNSET` | audio | The frozen coldboot baseline's FMV window (W23–W30 on the baseline's keys; the lanes agree through W141) hashes SILENCE where the console plays the movie audio: OLD pumped its mixer only at EE boundaries (`verify_boundary.cpp:58-72`) and no boundary passes during the movie, so OLD mixed the movie's PCM after the title's end-of-movie `BVOL = 0` (OLD's `[SND-PERF] rms=0` while `fmv_c` 11200 → 339200, i84b) — the title's BVOL path is intact on both trees. | Accepted difference (OLD defect) for the deterministic lane only if a per-field hashed pump is ever adopted (option (b), rejected today): under choice (a) the deterministic lane keeps the baseline's shape and value, so the row records the baseline's silence as OLD's, not as the console's; the play-mode device is the only consumer of the field pumps.**

## DP-30 — The title screen's blended-effects layer (orb additive effects, logo saturation, ocean specular): every draw group of the PCSX2 register stream is present in OLD's and NEW's DrawIR streams with an IDENTICAL blend / test / texture-function state, yet NEW's presented frame loses the bright pixels the dump's screenshot and OLD's frame carry — the gap is in NEW's lowering of the stream (render backend), not in the stream; evidence + options, no decision (2026-10-04, P8.20-B)

**Evidence** (`int_work/p820b/`). in every PCSX2 frame it is a zero-area strip — all 26 vertices at one point (`bbox=(2060,2059)-(2060,2059)`, rgba `00b2ff7f:18 20bfff7f:7 ffffff7f:1`) — so it has no pixel consequence in this frame; its cause is **OPEN / unattributed** (an object the title's state on PCSX2 carries at zero size that neither tree emits across 13 consecutive frames; not a phase difference, not measured further here).** Every blended key of the orb, ocean and tail is present in NEW with identical ALPHA/TEST/FBMSK/TFX/TCC/TEXA, as `[vs_textured_tristrip]` (class `world-tristrip` route) or `[raw]` sprites. (3) **The presented frames** (`region_stats.py`, `yellow_stats.py`; PCSX2 screenshot resized to 512x448; orientation: OLD's recorder PPM `old_title3/keep/f003000.ppm` is stored top-down (row 20 mean (206,143,156) = sky), NEW's `--dump-frames` PPM `new_cb3k/keep/f2800.ppm` bottom-up (row 20 (4,73,219) = water) — the helpers auto-detect per image and label the treatment; the NEW frame MEASURED is f2800, the NEW frame CENSUSED is b2519 (`title_2510_2519.gsdl` frame 9), both inside the stable title loop; boxes ORB (196,150)-(316,290), LOGO (104,110)-(416,236), OCEAN (0,318)-(512,388)): LOGO yellow pixels mean **PCSX2 (238,216,16)**, **OLD f3000 (238,216,14)**, **NEW f2800 (213,177,65)**; LOGO region `bright≥230` share PCSX2 0.605 / OLD 0.635 / **NEW 0.176**, saturation 148 / 153 / **113**; ORB bright share 0.417 / 0.488 / **0.109**; OCEAN bright share 0.659 / 0.640 / **0.455**, mean (66,120,204) / (35,103,224) / **(30,89,217)**, light pixels (R>120,G>150) 2,261 / 866–2,008 / **1,132**. OLD ≈ PCSX2 on every region; NEW loses the bright additive/specular pixels and its logo is lifted toward grey (B +49, R −25, G −39): the user's three defects reproduced in the deterministic headless frame (the same GL pipeline, scale 1).

**Where the mechanism is NOT** (from the census + the sources): (i) not an upstream drop — every group reaches the DrawIR sink (the `.gsdl` sits between the counting sink and the consumer, `ee_bootstrap.cpp:427-442`); (ii-a) not an unmapped blend combo — `blend_ff_map.cpp` maps `0101`, `0201`, `2001`, `0122`, `2021` exactly (`0201 → GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE`; `2001 → REVERSE_SUBTRACT`; `2021 → REVERSE_SUBTRACT, CONSTANT_ALPHA`), the same arms as OLD's `blend_ff_map.h` `MapAbcdToGlFF` with `subtractive_on`; FIX is 0 on every orb/ocean draw (only `[220] 0x80` and `[222] 0x20` use it); PABE=0 and COLCLAMP=1 everywhere but `[221]`; no `[GS-PARTRDR]`/declined counters apply (the draws are `vs_textured_tristrip` → `world-tristrip`, not `particles-untex`).

**Candidate mechanisms left (each a NEW-side lowering question; the evidence stage could not separate them in budget):** (A) the frame-tail post pass `[218]`–`[222]`: `[218]`'s alpha-only write (`fbmsk=0xffffff` on a CT16S target — a 16-bit frame mask is `fbmsk16_exact` territory), `[220]`'s T8H self-read with `tfx=3` and `ALPHA=0122` through the RT-serve path, `[221]`'s COLCLAMP=0 unblended write under `fbmsk=0xff808080`, and `[222]`'s full-screen `Cd − 0.25×Cs` from the CT16S RT with `TEXA ta0=0x80` through the shader-side blend (`gl_draw_executor.cpp:505-535` `using_rt` route) — a wrong small-buffer content there desaturates/darkens the WHOLE frame, which fits the logo's grey lift and the global loss of bright pixels; (B) the TFX modulate / alpha chain for the additive draws: texel alpha `ScaleAlphaPS2ToGPU` → inverse → `trunc(vc·tex/128)` → `×255/128` rescale (`gl_shader_sources.cpp:618-673, 893-923`) with vertex alpha 0x7f/0x26/0x00 — a double rescale or a clamp at 0x80 would halve/zero the additive contribution; (C) the depth test of the co-planar layers (`ztst=2` GEQUAL, the ocean specular `[183]` and water overlay `[174]` at the water's Z; `gl_shader_sources.cpp:64-68` "z quantizes to 128·Z … macOS GL4.1 lacks clip control") — a quantisation that orders the layer BELOW the base fails every specular pixel; (D) texture freshness for the per-frame re-uploaded slots (`0x3e0000` serves `[174]`, `[182]`, `[183]`, `[205]..[210]` within one frame with different uploads between them; the NEW recorder's `vram_snapshot_hash` is 0 — unmeasured). OLD renders all three correctly from the same stream, so OLD's `opengl_backend.cpp` lowering of exactly these keys is the oracle for each candidate.

**Options for the fix stage (P8.20-A):** (a) measure before fixing — replay the sliced NEW frame and OLD frame with `gs_replay` toggling each tail draw (`[218]`–`[222]`) off, and dump NEW's `0x230000` CT16S buffer and `0x70000` target before `[222]`, against OLD's `PS2_SNAP_RT=230000/70000`; the first toggle that restores the logo's yellow mean to (238,216,·) names the gap; (b) pin per candidate: a unit pin of `BlendFfMap` + the shader alpha chain against the PS2 equation `(A − B) × C + D` with `0x80 = 1.0` (ps2tek "Alpha blending"), a corpus pin of the title frame (the sliced `.gsdl` + the dump's screenshot as the reference image, `frame_diff.py` regions ORB/LOGO/OCEAN within the sub-step tolerance), and the title screen gated in the frames lane (`verify/run_leg.py` coldboot 2520 with the three region hashes); (c) a one-line toggle per landed change (Config key named in the DP-30 status paragraph), KEEP by the 12-leg score. **Recommendation:** (a) first, in the order A → B → C → D (A is the only candidate whose effect is frame-global, matching the logo's grey lift); then (b) with the dump's screenshot as the reference. **Row vs gap:** PCSX2 and OLD agree and NEW differs → a NEW construction gap (no §7.1 row); no OLD defect was found in this scene's stream or frame (the one PCSX2-only key `[181]` is a zero-area strip with no pixel consequence; its cause is OPEN / unattributed — see (2)).

**Status:** OPEN — evidence only; the P8.20-A fix stage decides. Toggle: none yet (no engine change in P8.20-B).

**DECISION (P8.20-A, 2026-10-04) — partial, MADE where measured:** (1) the ALPHA `0122` arm had no exact fixed-function entry (any decoded-texture `0122` draw fell to the alpha-over fallback) — the PS2 equation is exact in GL as `SUBTRACT(CONSTANT_ALPHA, CONSTANT_ALPHA)`; landed in `blend_ff_map.cpp` without a toggle (an exact arm replacing an approximation; `Render_BlendEquationPin` RED→GREEN, 35 checks). CORRECTED by P8.21-A (the P8.20-A review's MAJOR 3): on the title, `[220]` takes the sw-blend route (`int_work/p820a/leg_trace/draw_routes.txt:3976` `ALPHA=0122 … owner=surface fmt=4 … blend=sw rtcopy=1 copied=1`), so the arm has no effect on the title frame — P8.20-A's `leg_base` and `leg_trace` f2519 frames are byte-identical (`cmp` silent). Release status (MAJOR 4): P8.20-A did not rebuild Release; the reviewer rebuilt it (`build-release/app/host/nativeee` 19:39, rc 0, 0 warnings) and re-ran `RootReleaseLanesIdentical` → `guard=IDENTICAL`. (2) Candidates A and the route half of D are RULED OUT by measurement, not reasoning: `DrawRouteTrace` (`int_work/p820a/leg_trace/draw_routes.txt`, the title frame b2519, 3,981 draws) shows every orb / ocean / logo draw `owner=decoded blend=ff` (the exact fixed-function arms from decoded textures — no render-target serve, no shader blend, no destination copy); the tail `[222] ALPHA=2021 TEX=230000 owner=surface blend=sw` IS present and the static scene (sky (229,163,137) vs PCSX2 (219,157,136); cliffs (155,132,93) vs (158,134,91); water base (19,86,212) vs (27,94,215)) matches — a frame-global pass would move the sky. (3) The deviation is LOCAL to the letters and the orb: yellow letters (232,213,13)→(220,188,69), red (193,86,31)→(129,79,63), orb centre (49,49,113)→(10,10,35); the two letter shifts are inconsistent with any single overlay colour and additive draws cannot lower a channel — the letters' OWN texels are wrong (texel addressing/content of the decoded T8 atlas on the STQ path: `UvRegion` sub-rect rebase, native-centre requantisation, the mip LOD floor) and/or the glow's Z against the letters (C). Next: the texture-side pin (decode + STQ addressing of a 32x32 T8 atlas vs the dump's IMAGE bytes) then C. Measured outcome: regions unchanged by (1) (the coldboot frame has no `0122` draw before the tail; frames lane keys ≤ 68 untouched by construction — no `0122` in them). Status after P8.20-A: OPEN (narrowed), BUG-080.

**DECISION (P8.21-A, 2026-10-04) — the lowering is EXONERATED, the gap moves upstream of the render backend:** the dump's frame 1 — all 135 host→local uploads and 225 draws in stream order, the console's own bytes (`verify/baseline/title_screen/pcsx2_start_screen_f1.gsf1`, `tests/render/test_title_frame_replay.cpp`) — through NEW's TransferEngine → VramImage → DrawPipeline (resolver, class renderers) → GlDevice reproduces the console's image: LOGO yellow (241,217,19) bright 0.639 / ORB bright 0.470 / OCEAN bright 0.753 vs PCSX2 (238,216,16) 0.605 / 0.417 / 0.659 (gate `Render_TitleFrameReplay`, red when the logo draw is skipped: (183,142,93)). Candidates B (TFX/alpha chain), C (Z of co-planar layers), the mip LOD floor, the STQ addressing and the decoder are therefore NOT the mechanism (pins `Texsrc_TitleAtlasDecode`, `Render_TitleLogoDrawPin`). For the live run the inputs were compared one by one: the logo draw's vertices/STQ/RGBAQ/TEX0 in NEW's own recording equal the dump's; the texture bytes in NEW's RAM equal the dump's upload (8192/8192); NEW's VRAM page 0x3c0000 at the frame boundary equals the dump's upload as stored (8192/8192, savestate `state_b2519.ps2st`). The live chain past those inputs has two links and loop 1 (the P8.21-A review's MAJOR 1) MEASURED both: (A) the composition into 0x70000, read back live at key 2519 (`f2519_drawn_0x70000.ppm`, a census readback added to `--dump-frames`; the frames lane is byte-identical with it): LOGO yellow (219,183,67) bright 0.620 / ORB 0.303 / OCEAN 0.536 — the defect is already in the composition, and the letter fill is ONE flat colour (231,199,73) on 16959/16968 letter pixels = the texel (116,100,37) under the correct ×255>>7 modulate, a word that no CLUT in the live VRAM/RAM or in any upload of the frame holds (the correct CLUT IS in the live VRAM at 0x3c04c4) and that no box mean of the atlas equals — a computed colour (level/filter/program state), not a consumed byte; (B) the display copy 0x70000 → FBP 0 CT16: live it is the PSMCT16 truncation exactly (`src & 0xF8` bright 0.186 vs displayed 0.182), as in the replay (`--repeat 2 --scanout 0` → (236,213,15) / 0.485 / 0.733 = the console; gate `Render_TitleFrameReplayDisplay`) — the display's bright-share collapse is a threshold effect of (A) (43.6 % of the live box at max channel 230–231 → 224). A `--uploads-first` replay does not reproduce the live numbers either ((236,211,23)). The open class is therefore link A — the colour the live logo draw SAMPLES (the decode cache's generation, the mip levels the live `MipSampler` captures from VRAM, or inherited GL sampler/program state) — not the EE inputs, not the lowering of the dump's state, not the display copy (BUG-081, OPEN, narrowed with numbers). One construction gap found and fixed on the way with a RED→GREEN pin: `VramImage::ReadView` overlaid a pending GPU deposit over a newer EE transfer (BUG-082, `Gs_Inv39_ViewHonoursNewerWriter`); measured outcome: the title frame hash is unchanged (not the mechanism), lanes intact. Toggle: none (construction gap, no behaviour choice). Status after P8.21-A: OPEN (link A: the live-sampled texel colour), with the seconds-scale instruments landed (`Render_TitleFrameReplay`, `Render_TitleFrameReplayDisplay`, the `_drawn_` census readback).

**DECISION (P8.24-A, 2026-10-05) — ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED (BUG-084); BUG-081 CLOSED.** The stage first tested the prompt's hypothesis (a pending Z→CT16 writeback deposit of `[168]/[169]` overlaid on the atlas page when `[167]` decodes) with a new census, the `TexresTrace` (`engine/gs/hle/texsrc/texres_trace.*`; diagnostics builds, `<dump-frames dir>/texres_trace.cfg` with `tbp=`/`cbp=`/`bytes=1`/`from=`; one record per resolve of the filtered reads: the tier / cache outcome, the content key, the deferred entries the flush took, and per 8 KiB page of the window and the CLUT the commit state, author, upload / shadow epochs and whether the view substituted the pending shadow; with `bytes=1` the window + CLUT bytes and the BOUND texture's level 0 are kept): in frame 2519 the 78 logo strips read a window byte-identical to the dump's atlas page as the GS stores it (md5 `13f03492a4857d0420891e843f161a4f`), every page `clean author=cpu overlay=0`, `flushed=0` — the hypothesis is REFUTED — and bound a GL texture whose level 0 equals the engine's own decode of the dump's page (78/78 identical). The same record printed the live `TEX1(mxl=2 lcm=0 l=0 k=4027)`: K is the RAW 12-bit field, where the dump's `0xfbb` is −69 (s7.4 = −4.3125); the replay harnesses sign-extend K in their own `ViewFromRegs`, the live `DrawFlush::FillTexture` did not (`t.k = static_cast<int16_t>(c.tex1.k)`). With a +251.7 bias the shader's retail LOD term clamps at MXL: the letters sampled level 2 (8x8), where their flat yellow swatch is one blended texel — the box mean of that neighbourhood ((117.5,98.5,39.2) in the synthesized 4x4 tail, `synth_levels.py`) is the live flat texel (116,100,37) that no CLUT holds. OLD sign-extends (`host_app/gs_mipmap.h:252-254`). Options: (a) sign-extend in `FillTexture` (the GS rule 3.4.12, the `TexReadView` contract, OLD); (b) a toggle to the unsigned read — rejected: the unsigned read is a defect with no hardware or title rationale. Choice: (a), no toggle (construction gap, pre-authorized). Pins: `Gs_Inv40_Tex1KSignExtended` (RED before: 2 failures), the live gate `RootTitleScreenEffects` (the Release coldboot 2520 → frame 2519's regions vs the console; RED on the pre-fix frame — three FAIL lines; PASS live, 93.6 s; no resumable title image needed), `Render_TitleLogoDrawPin` now carries the dump's MIPTBP1 chain ((242,217,19) n=20870, closer to the console than the garbage-level chain's (250,230,14)) and a `--split` ablation (78-vertex draws: identical result).
